### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 6 are applied in this PR (plan text, config, `.gitignore`); 7 to 9 are notes for the tasks named.

1. **`next build` doesn't lint in Next 16.** T08's Verify said "type or lint errors fail it (no `ignoreBuildErrors`)", and so did the prompt. Measured: a type error fails the build (`Failed to type check.`, exit 1), a lint error doesn't (exit 0), because Next 16 removed ESLint from `next build` along with `next lint` and the `eslint` config option (the ESLint page of the docs says so). The plan's Verify now says that; lint errors are caught by `pnpm lint` (root) and by CI (T16), which must run `lint` as its own step. I did not put `eslint .` in the `build` script: the root `lint` already runs it, and it would run twice.
2. **A single unknown `[market]` segment gets Next's default 404, not `global-not-found`.** `/does-not-exist` matches `/[market]`, the root layout calls `notFound()`, and the framework's built-in not-found renders (`<html id="__next_error__">`, no `lang`, status 404). `global-not-found` is what Next renders for a URL that matches no route (measured: `/`, `/a/b`, `/el-cy/nope`), and for a server action that is not found (`createNotFoundLoaderTree` in `next/dist/server/app-render/app-render.js`); a `notFound()` thrown inside a matched route goes to that route's not-found boundary, and for the root layout that is the framework's default. **For T11:** as planned, the proxy rewrites every path its matcher lets through to an internal `/{market-key}/…` path, so `[market]` only receives a key the proxy chose, and a stray `/does-not-exist` would become `/el-cy/does-not-exist`, which matches no route and gets `global-not-found`. The default page can only be reached by something that skips the proxy. If T11 wants its own page there anyway, a `not-found.tsx` in `(storefront)/` (above the root layout) is the file to add.
3. **`typedRoutes` makes `href="/"` a type error** (`Type '"/"' is not assignable to type 'UrlObject | RouteImpl<"/">'`), because `/` is no file route: only `/[market]` is. `global-not-found.tsx` casts it (`"/" as Route`, `import type { Route } from "next"`), and the market's `not-found.tsx` links to `/${market.key}`, which type-checks and works today. **For T11:** public URLs come from `buildPublicUrl`, which should return `Route` so no component casts.
4. **No `transpilePackages`.** Next's bundled docs say Turbopack transpiles workspace packages (npm, pnpm and Yarn) automatically, and the hot-reload check shows `packages/i18n` source compiled and reloaded without it. `experimental.globalNotFound` is still the flag's name in 16.4 (`config-shared.d.ts`, `config-schema.js`; the page on nextjs.org still says "experimental").
5. **`next dev` writes an `AGENTS.md` into the app** (`apps/storefront/AGENTS.md`, `✓ Generated AGENTS.md for AI agents`). It is the managed block `<!-- BEGIN:nextjs-agent-rules -->` that sends an agent to `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` (the same idea as turbo's block in the root `AGENTS.md`), and its text asks to be committed. It is committed here. `agentRules: false` in `next.config.ts` opts out (and removes the block on the next `next dev`); I left it on, since the bundled docs are what the plan's "read the installed source" advice points at. **For T09:** the file sits in the app folder, so `withPayload` and `payload.config.ts` don't care.
6. **`next-env.d.ts` is git-ignored** (root `.gitignore`, under "Build output"). `next typegen`, `next dev` and `next build` all write it, with different `.next/types` and `.next/dev/types` imports, and a fresh clone's `typecheck` creates it first (`next typegen && tsc --noEmit`). Files T01 listed gained one line.
7. **`lib/shell-markets.ts` is a stand-in for T10.** The layout needs the market keys and each key's `<html lang>` before T10's library exists, so `listShellMarkets()` derives them from `@shopblok/fixtures` (`{language}-{country}` of each enabled country; `htmlLang` override or `{language}-{COUNTRY}`; a language without a catalog in `@shopblok/i18n` gets English). `current-market.ts` reads the root param and calls `notFound()` for an unknown key. **T10 deletes both** and the layout reads `getMarketMap()`; its tests (`shell-markets.test.ts`, 6) show what the stand-in did. T10 also needs to know that the layout is `async` and calls `getCurrentMarket()` before rendering `<html>`.
8. **The `.env.example` lists variables nothing reads yet** (`NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_MEDUSA_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, the names the spec and T13 use), marked as such in the file. T09 reads the first, T12 the other two through `src/lib/env.ts`. `turbo.json`'s `build.env` already lists `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL`.
9. **`images.dangerouslyAllowLocalIP` is set from the config function's `phase`** (`PHASE_DEVELOPMENT_SERVER` from `next/constants`), so `next.config.ts` is a function and reads no `process.env`. T09's `withPayload(nextConfig, …)` takes the object that function returns: wrap it as `(phase) => withPayload(build(phase), {...})`, or call the function once.

### Backend decisions from T07 (done in T08)

Stephanos answered T07's three open questions on 2026-10-09 (store name, the search consumer, currencies). All three touch the backend, so this PR does them, with the stack (Docker, `pnpm infra:up`) started for it.

1. **The store is named `Shopbloks Starter`** (`STORE_NAME` in `apps/backend/src/config/markets.ts`; the spelling is as given, and it is one constant if `Shopblok Starter` was meant). `seed-markets.ts` sets it only while the store still has Medusa's default `Medusa Store` (or none), so an admin's rename survives a re-run (`storeName` in `lib/plan.ts`, unit-tested). On a wiped stack: run 1 `Updated store …: name, currencies, locales`, `select name from store` → `Shopbloks Starter`; run 2 `Store … is up to date`. After `update store set name='Acme Shop'` a run said `up to date` and the name stayed; after `set name='Medusa Store'` it said `Updated store …: name` and the name was `Shopbloks Starter` again.
2. **The shared-option consumer is fixed** (`src/search/helpers/resolve-product-ids.ts`). In 2.21.2 `product_option` has `products` (many to many, since 2.16) and **no `product_id` at all**, so the upstream branches for `product-option` and `product-option-value` never worked; the fix reads `products.id` and `option.products.id`, and skips null rows. Before and after, on a live stack with the local event bus, renaming the shared option `Color` (6 products): with the **original** consumer the log said `An error occurred while processing product-option.updated: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'product_id')` and the 6 search documents kept the old title (`Hue:Black` in 6, `Color:Black` in 0); with the **fixed** one the 6 documents changed (`Hue:Black` in 6 after `Colour → Hue`). `seed-demo-catalog.ts` now logs **0** `error` lines on a wiped stack (T07: 2), in both runs. With Redis, the first dev-server boot drained a `product-option.updated` job queued earlier by a `medusa exec` run, and the documents went from `Hue:Black` to `Color:Black` (the option's name at that moment). Not checked: deleting a shared option (Medusa detaches its products when it deletes the option, so they may not be findable from the event; the next product event reindexes them).
3. **Currencies, regions and languages: one file, and the search index follows it.** `apps/backend/src/config/markets.ts` (new; `scripts/lib/markets.ts` re-exports it) holds `SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES`, `MARKET_REGIONS`, `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` and the store name. `seed-markets.ts` reads it, and so does the search index: `PRICE_CURRENCIES` in `search/helpers/pricing.ts` was `["eur", "usd"]` and is now the store's currencies, so `min_price_gbp`, `min_price_chf` and the rest exist (`max_`, `original_`, `on_sale_`). The demo catalog prices itself in every supported currency (EUR price × `DEMO_RATES_PER_EUR`, rounded, for a currency a product doesn't list). The file's header says how to open a market. What the owner asked is split by task: this PR does the backend half; the rest is written into the plan as decision D17, with an owner for each part (plan §1).

   Checked on a wiped stack with the committed config (EUR, USD, GBP, CHF):

   | Check | Result |
   |---|---|
   | `/store/search` filters by price in GBP and CHF | `min_price_gbp` `$lte 12` → Ceramic Mug (12), Dotted Notebook (10); `min_price_chf` `$gte 40` → Everyday Joggers (48), Heritage Hoodie (58); `$gte 20, $lte 30` → Classic Tee (26), Canvas Cap (22) |
   | Sort and range facet | `pagination.order {min_price_gbp: ASC}` → Dotted Notebook (10), Ceramic Mug (12), Everyday Tote Bag (16); `facets [{field: min_price_gbp, type: stats}]` → `{"min":10,"max":51,"avg":25.875,"count":8}`; the same for `chf` (`min` 12, `max` 58) |
   | Both seeds twice, 151 tables | Run 2 changed **0 of 151** tables (`count(*)` of every table, diffed); 0 `error` lines in all four runs |

   And with Brazil and Mongolia added to the file (`brl` and `mnt` currencies, regions `Brazil` and `Mongolia`, locales `pt-BR` and `mn-MN`; edit reverted afterwards, not in the PR), on a wiped stack:

   | Check | Result |
   |---|---|
   | Seed output | `Created regions: Europe, United States, United Kingdom, Switzerland, Brazil, Mongolia`, `Created tax regions: cy, de, fr, us, gb, ch, br, mn`, six service zones and six "Standard" options |
   | `/store/regions`, `/store/locales` | `Brazil brl br`, `Mongolia mnt mn`; `en-US el-GR de-DE fr-FR pt-BR mn-MN` |
   | Price of the demo tee per region | `chf 26`, `eur 25`, `brl 155`, `usd 28`, `mnt 92500`, `gbp 22` |
   | Search in the new currencies | `min_price_brl` `$lte 100` → Dotted Notebook (74), Ceramic Mug (87); `min_price_mnt` `$gte 150000` → Everyday Joggers, Heritage Hoodie; sort by `min_price_brl` DESC and the `stats` facet (`min` 74, `max` 366) work |
   | Language | `x-medusa-locale: el-GR` gives `Κλασικό μπλουζάκι`; `pt-BR` and `mn-MN` give `Classic Tee` (no translations, so English) |
   | Money formatting | `formatMoney(1234.5, "BRL", "pt-BR")` → `R$ 1.234,50`, `MNT` in `mn-MN` → `₮ 1,234.50` (two new rows in the i18n tests, 18 pass) |

   **Adding a currency to a database that exists** (before, the file's comment and upstream's said "the module reindexes on the next boot"): that is only true after `medusa db:migrate`. Measured: after the edit, the dev server restarted and the index stayed at version 2. `db:migrate` then printed `Migrating search indexes…` / `Search indexes migrated. They are filled when the application starts` and created version 3 (`pending`); the next start logged `[Search] Seeding "product" (schema_changed) into "product_v3"`, `Making "product" version 3 active`, and the old table `search_pg_product_v2` stayed beside `search_pg_product_v3`. `pnpm bootstrap` (T13) runs `db:migrate`, so it covers this; a developer who only edits the file and restarts does not. Also: **demo products that exist already keep their prices**; a currency added later gets demo prices only on a fresh database (the seed creates products, it doesn't reconcile them).
4. **Medusa requires every file under `src/search/` when the app boots.** My first version of the new tests sat in `src/search/helpers/__tests__/`; `jest`, `medusa build` and `medusa lint` passed, but `medusa db:migrate` and the dev server crashed on boot with `ReferenceError: describe is not defined` (the Search Module loads each file in `src/search` as an index definition). The tests live in `src/config/__tests__/` now (`search-pricing.unit.spec.ts`, `search-resolve-product-ids.unit.spec.ts`), with a comment in each. **No test file under `src/search/`**; the same may hold for the other folders Medusa loads (`src/api`, `src/modules`, `src/subscribers`, `src/jobs`, `src/workflows`, `src/links`): put tests next to nothing there. Only a real boot shows it. The first `db:migrate` crashed after it had run the migrations, and a second one hung on a lock left by the first (`idle in transaction`); I killed it by PID and the third run exited 0.
5. **Tests:** the backend has 85 (64 before): `storeName` and the `name` drift (3), `demoPrices` and rates (6), the config/markets checks (2), the search currencies (4) and `resolveProductIds` (6). Each of four mutations failed the right test and was reverted: the search currencies back to `["eur", "usd"]` (3 failed), the consumer back to `product_id` (1), `storeName` always renaming (1), the demo rate ignored (2).

### Smaller observations

- **The permission check declined two commands once** (`[Modify Shared Resources]`): `cp -n apps/backend/.env.example apps/backend/.env; pnpm … medusa db:migrate`, and then a read-only `sed` of two backend files. The same migration ran when I gave `DATABASE_URL` and `REDIS_URL` in the command and copied no `.env` (the database is the compose stack's, on `localhost:15432`). All backend runs here pass those two variables the same way and there is no `apps/backend/.env`. T09 needs the stack for Payload's database and may meet the same prompt; an allow rule for the compose and `medusa` commands in the cloud environment's settings would avoid it.
- **`next dev` first request 4.07 s, then 0.12 s** (`Ready in 629ms`); `next start` `Ready in 162 ms`. The first build compiled in 3.5 s, and 0.5 s from the cache.
- **`next dev` ran with `Cache Components enabled`, `Partial Prefetching enabled`** and printed no warning. A page that calls `notFound()` makes dev log `Could not validate 'instant' because an error prevented the target segment from rendering` (three times for the probe route); it is the dev-only instant-navigation validation seeing the 404, harmless.
- **`tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`** appears in `apps/storefront` (the Next preset sets `incremental`); `.gitignore` covers `*.tsbuildinfo`.
- **The Greek, German and French `notFound` copy is mine** and has not been checked by a native speaker (as with the other catalogs, T03 item 3).
- `pnpm install` still prints `[WARN] deprecated eslint@9.39.5` (T02 item 1; decided), and five deprecated subdependencies from the backend (T05 item 5).

## Payload embedded (T09)

**Result: every check in T09's Verify list passes. T10 can start.** Payload 3.90.2 is embedded in `apps/storefront`: `/admin` shows create-first-user, then login and logout work; `GET /api/pages` returns 200 with no drafts; an API key authenticates a machine user at `/api/integrations/me`; `payload migrate` applies the saved initial migration to an empty `payload` database in 191 ms; `generate:types` and `generate:importmap` leave no diff; and `pnpm check:workspace` has its first real Payload lines. Three things differ from what the plan assumed, and each cost a fix: the Payload packages needed a pnpm `packageExtensions` entry to get the storefront's React (item 1), Payload's generated migration doesn't type-check or load under the repo's `verbatimModuleSyntax` (item 2), and my first `markets` validator rejected every save (item 3, found by the live check, not by a test).

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 6 are applied in this PR (plan text, config); 7 to 10 are notes for the tasks named.

1. **Payload's packages need a `packageExtensions` entry, or their React is the backend's (D6).** `@payloadcms/next` declares neither `react` nor `@types/react` (92 of its files import `react`); `@payloadcms/ui` and `@payloadcms/richtext-lexical` declare `react` as a peer but not `@types/react`. pnpm resolves what a package doesn't declare from its shared hoist folder, `node_modules/.pnpm/node_modules`, and that held `react@18.3.1` and `@types/react@18.3.31`: `apps/backend` comes before `apps/storefront` and both depend on React directly, so the backend's wins. Symptoms: `tsc` failed on the template's own file (`(payload)/layout.tsx(27,5): error TS2322: Type 'ReactNode' is not assignable to type 'React.ReactNode'`, a React 19 child against React 18's `ReactNode`), and `@payloadcms/next` resolved `react` 18.3.1 (`check:workspace` showed it). Next's bundler aliases `react` for app-router code, which is why the admin rendered in T00; the resolution matters for `tsc`, for code Node runs and for file tracing. T00's embed built, so its tree evidently didn't hoist the 18 types ahead of 19 (not checked why). **Fix:** `packageExtensions` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` adds `react` and `react-dom` as peers of `@payloadcms/next`, and `@types/react` and `@types/react-dom` as optional peers of all three; they then link to the storefront's 19.3.0. Keyed by bare package name, so it keeps applying after an upgrade (and is harmless if Payload fixes it). T00's fallback `publicHoistPattern: ["@payloadcms/*"]` was not needed and not tried. **`check:workspace` now also checks `@payloadcms/next` and `@payloadcms/richtext-lexical`**; with the entry removed it fails with `FAIL  apps/storefront → @payloadcms/next: react 18.3.1, expected 19.3.0` (exit 1), and with it restored every line is `ok`. `pnpm peers check` is otherwise as in T08, plus one new line for the storefront: an unmet **optional** peer `esbuild` of `vite@8.3.3` (`peerDependenciesMeta.esbuild.optional`), because Payload's tooling brings esbuild 0.25.12; Vitest runs fine.
2. **Payload's generated migration doesn't survive `verbatimModuleSyntax` (T02's base tsconfig).** `payload migrate:create` writes `import { MigrateUpArgs, MigrateDownArgs, sql } from '@payloadcms/db-postgres'` from a template hard-coded in `@payloadcms/drizzle` (`getMigrationTemplate.js`, no option to change it). Both names are types. `tsc` says `TS1484: 'MigrateUpArgs' is a type and must be imported using a type-only import` (twice), and `payload migrate` itself failed to load the file: `SyntaxError: The requested module '@payloadcms/db-postgres' does not provide an export named 'MigrateDownArgs'`, because the CLI runs through `tsx`, which applies the app's tsconfig (so it keeps the import) to files that tsconfig includes. The templates don't see this: their tsconfig has no `verbatimModuleSyntax`. **Fix:** `src/migrations` is in `exclude` in the storefront's `tsconfig.json` (tsx then transforms those files with its defaults, which drop unused imports) and in `globalIgnores` in `eslint.config.mjs`. They are generated and not imported by the app (the config passes no `prodMigrations`). Every later `migrate:create` is committed as it is written.
3. **A custom `validate` replaces Payload's own check, `required` included.** My first `countryCode` validator (for `markets`) returned an error for an empty optional `primaryCountry`, so `POST /api/globals/markets` gave 400 for every body; the unit tests I had at that point didn't touch it, and the browser check did. It now calls Payload's `text` validator first, accepts an empty optional value, then checks the shape, and `globals/markets.test.ts` covers the four cases. T10's `medusaLocale` validator must do the same (`import { text } from "payload/shared"`).
4. **`blank` at v3.90.2 does have a committed `admin/importMap.js`.** T00's item 5 said it has none. The tarball in `/opt/payload-templates/blank` holds one (270 bytes, only `CollectionCards`). It is not what this app's `payload generate:importmap` writes (same entry, different quoting); the generated file is committed, so the "no git diff" check holds. The route files (`layout.tsx`, `admin/[[...segments]]/{page,not-found}.tsx`, `api/[...slug]/route.ts`, `custom.scss`) are byte for byte the template's (`cmp`), and the two `(payload)` folders of `blank` and `with-postgres` are identical. `api/graphql` and `api/graphql-playground` are left out, and `graphQL: { disable: true }` is set.
5. **The `markets` global is built in full here, not in T10 (D12, T10 step 1).** `filterAvailableLocales` and S3 need its shape, and a second migration for T10 would be noise: `countries[]` (`code` lowercase ISO 3166-1, `enabled`, `languages` as a multi-select of the 42, `domain`), `languages[]` (`code`, `medusaLocale`, `htmlLang`), `primaryCountry`, `fallbackMarket` and an empty `synced` group, as T10 describes. It is in the initial migration. The plan's T10 text now says so: T10 adds the `medusaLocale` validation against Medusa's 51 codes, the library and the tests, and changes the schema only if it has to (then it is a second saved migration). **`languages` as a multi-select stores an array of strings, which is the shape of `MarketMap.languages` in `@shopblok/fixtures`**, so `getMarketMap()` maps almost one to one.
6. **The 42 languages are in four enums, not one (D17, S3 L10).** The migration holds `_locales`, `enum__pages_v_published_locale`, `enum_markets_countries_languages` and `enum_markets_languages_code`, each with the 42 codes (`select` fields built from `LANGUAGES` copy the list). A language outside the list therefore touches four enums; `migrate:create` writes all four by itself, but S3's runbook (L10) should say it. `locales.ts` is the one list. The 42: the EU's 24, plus Medusa's 36 languages (51 locale codes), plus `ar ja nb ru tr uk zh`, which Medusa already covers, so the union is exactly 42 (the plan said "about 42"). `locales.test.ts` reads Medusa's `defaults.js` from the pnpm store and fails if a Medusa release adds a language that isn't preloaded. Migration size at 42: `20261009_115513_initial.ts` **16,473 bytes**, the `.json` snapshot **57,479 bytes**, 191 ms to run (S3 compares with a 4-locale config).
7. **For S1:** `/admin/[[...segments]]` is `◐` in the build. In `next dev` the admin logs three Next errors per page load in the browser console: `Could not validate that a segment in your UI has instant navigation` (the dropped segment is `admin/[[...segments]]/page.tsx`; Next offers `export const instant = false`, which is the plan's W1), `encountered the unstable value new Date() while prerendering` (the plan's #18065 family; that issue is about `Date.now()`, here it is `new Date()`) and `encountered URL data in generateMetadata()`. The login, logout, create-first-user and locale checks passed in spite of them. Also `/admin/nope` returns 200, and `⨯ turbopackServerFastRefresh` is in the banner as in T00. Dev timing: `/admin` first request 36.6 s by `curl` (Next's log says `GET /admin 200 in 10.5s` after the compile line), `/api/pages` 7.7 s, `/el-cy` 2.3 s, then milliseconds.
8. **For T13 (`bootstrap`):** `apps/storefront/.env` needs `PAYLOAD_SECRET` (a long random string) and, if not the defaults, `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`; `.env.example` lists them and leaves `PAYLOAD_SECRET` empty on purpose (Payload stops with `missing secret key` rather than run with a shared secret). Then `pnpm --filter @shopblok/storefront payload migrate`. `payload migrate` reads `.env` itself (the CLI runs from the app's folder).
9. **For T11 and T12:** the `payload` Local API works from server code in this app (the admin and REST routes use it); T10's `getMarketMap()` reads the global with `payload.findGlobal({ slug: "markets" })`, which is not subject to access (Local API default), while the REST read of `markets` is staff-only. `serverURL`, `cors` and `csrf` are all `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, and a cookie-authenticated write from any other origin is refused (403, checked with `https://evil.example`). If T11 serves a country on its own domain (`markets.domain`), the admin and REST API are still the main origin's: that is intended, and a storefront page on another domain must not call them with a cookie.
10. **Decisions left to the owner** (none blocks T10): see "Smaller observations", the last four items. **Decided in the T10 session: the plan's D18 to D23** ("Markets library (T10)", "Owner decisions").

### Smaller observations

- **The access model, in one place** (`src/payload/access/roles.ts`, 9 tests): `users` have a `role` of `admin` or `editor` (a field only an admin can change); the very first account is made an admin by a `beforeChange` hook, whatever the form says (the create-first-user form does show the field, with `Editor` selected and a description that says so), and every later account is made by an admin. `integrations` are machine users with an API key and no password (`auth: { useAPIKey: true, disableLocalStrategy: true }`, role `medusa-sync`); they can read only themselves, can't open the admin panel, and every check looks at `req.user.collection` first, because an integration with id 1 is not the staff member with id 1. `pages` and `media` are staff-write; `media` is public-read, `pages` public-read of published documents only; `markets` is staff-read and admin-write. The version history of `pages` is staff-only too (`readVersions`; left unset, Payload lets any signed-in user through, an integration's key included), and the last admin can be neither demoted nor deleted (`keepOneAdmin`, `keepOneAdminOnDelete`). The plan's rule for Local API calls (`overrideAccess: false` plus `user`) is for calls made on behalf of a user. T09 makes two Local API calls, both system reads that pass `overrideAccess: true` with a comment saying why: the user count in the first-user hook (nobody is signed in yet) and the `markets` read in `filterAvailableLocales` (which languages exist is the shop's setting, so an editor who may not read `markets` still gets the same selector).
- **Cookies and lockout:** `users` lock after 5 failed logins for 10 minutes; the cookie is `Secure` when `NODE_ENV === "production"`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`. In production mode `curl`'s cookie jar still sent the `Secure` cookie to `http://localhost` (the CSRF check above used it); scripted calls here used the JWT (`Authorization: JWT <token>` from `/api/users/login`) otherwise.
- **Admin console and network noise in the cloud:** `HTTP 400 POST /api/users/logout` when `/admin/logout` is opened (the page has already logged out on the server, then the client calls the endpoint with no session; harmless), aborted prefetches (`REQUEST FAILED … /admin/login`) during redirects, and a failed image `www.gravatar.com/avatar/…` (the admin shows a broken avatar here because that host isn't allowed). The avatar request is Payload's default and sends a hash of the user's email to Gravatar; `admin.avatar: "default"` turns it off. Not changed.
- **First `pnpm install` 26.5 s and the second 6.9 s**; pnpm reused 1326 packages from the store and downloaded 249.
- **A required field with `admin.condition` becomes nullable.** I tried hiding `users.role` on the create-first-user form that way; `migrate:create` then wrote `ALTER TABLE "users" ALTER COLUMN "role" DROP NOT NULL`, and `payload-types.ts` made `role` optional. A function `defaultValue` would instead drop the column's `DEFAULT 'editor'`. Neither is worth a form label, so the field stays always shown and says in its description that the first account is an admin; the stray migration was deleted and the types regenerated.
- **Payload telemetry is on** (its default; `telemetry: false` in the config turns it off). The cloud's allowed domains don't include its host, so nothing was sent from here. CLOUD.md turns the other tools' telemetry off, so this is the one remaining default; not changed, owner's call.
- **Creating a missing database on connect:** off in production, on in development. `disableCreateDatabase` is `NODE_ENV === "production"` (from the review), so a deploy that forgot `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` fails on connect instead of creating `payload` on whichever Postgres answers on localhost. In development it stays on, because the plan says Payload creates it and T13's preflight may count on that; `disableCreateDatabase: true` for every environment is the one line the owner can still choose (D15's hazard: a port clash sends the connection to another project's server). The compose stack creates `payload` itself, so nothing needs the creation.
- **Store name:** Stephanos confirmed `Shopbloks Starter` (with an s) in T09's prompt; `STORE_NAME` in `apps/backend/src/config/markets.ts` is unchanged.
- **The `payload` script runs `payload` directly**; the templates prefix `NODE_OPTIONS=--no-deprecation` through `cross-env`. No deprecation warning appeared on Node 22.22.0, so `cross-env` was not added.
- `pnpm install` still prints `[WARN] deprecated eslint@9.39.5` (T02 item 1; decided) and now seven deprecated subdependencies (T08's note said five, from the backend, T05 item 5): `@esbuild-kit/core-utils`, `@esbuild-kit/esm-loader`, `cron-parser`, `glob@7.2.3`, `inflight`, `lodash.isequal`, `uuid@9.0.1`. I did not trace which come from Payload.
- **Changes from the review of this PR.** A code review of the diff made 12 points. Each fix below was checked on the built app against Postgres, not only in unit tests. (a) **`pages` had no `readVersions` rule,** so Payload's default applied (`executeAccess` lets any signed-in principal through when the rule is unset). Reproduced first: with an `integrations` API key, `GET /api/pages/versions` gave **200** with `totalDocs 2` (the draft included) and `/api/pages/versions/1` gave 200. With `readVersions: isStaff`: **403** for both, 200 for an admin, 403 for a visitor. The same default applies to Payload's `payload-locked-documents`; I found no setting for it, and a key can only create and delete edit locks there. (b) **The last admin could demote or delete themself,** and with no user left the next visitor to `/admin/create-first-user` becomes an admin. `keepOneAdmin` (`beforeChange`) and `keepOneAdminOnDelete` (`beforeDelete`) refuse when no other admin remains. Checked live: the only admin's `PATCH` of their own role and `DELETE` both give 400 *"This is the last admin. Make another user an admin first."*; after a second admin is added the demotion works and the remaining admin is protected again. The live check also showed that a bare `APIError` came back as *"An unknown error occurred."* from the production build: `formatErrors` needs the error's class name or its `data`, and minification empties the name. The error now carries `data: {}`. The role field's description no longer promises that the first account is the only thing it does. (c) **`locales.test.ts` read another workspace's install, which turbo's cache for `storefront#test` could not see.** It now stops unless exactly one `@medusajs/translation` is installed (it took the first), and 51 is a floor, not an equality. `apps/storefront/turbo.json` adds `$TURBO_ROOT$/pnpm-lock.yaml` to that task's `inputs`: touching the lockfile changed only `@shopblok/storefront#test`'s hash in `turbo run test --dry=json`, and the default inputs are still there. (d) **`filterAvailableLocales` treated any error as "no markets enabled".** Now only Postgres `42P01` (`undefined_table`, which drizzle puts on `error.cause`; checked against the real error from a database with no tables) does; anything else fails the request. (e) **`turbo.json` `dev` has `passThroughEnv: ["PAYLOAD_*"]`.** Strict mode dropped `PAYLOAD_DB_PUSH=true pnpm dev` and a `PAYLOAD_SECRET` from the shell; `turbo run dev --dry=json` now lists both under `passthrough`. `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is covered by turbo's Next.js inference, and a value in `.env` always worked. (f) **The production database guard** (the item above): with `NODE_ENV=production` and a missing database, `cannot connect to Postgres: database "probe_production" does not exist` and no database was created; with `development`, it was created (then dropped). (g) **`markets` builds its language options from `LOCALES`,** which already holds each label (42 `Intl.DisplayNames` fewer at config load); `migrate:create --skip-empty` still created no file. `locales.test.ts` also checks that `DEFAULT_LOCALE` and every store language in `@shopblok/i18n` exist here, so the two lists cannot drift apart unseen. (h) **`check:workspace` now checks `@types/react` as well** (19 for the storefront and for `@payloadcms/next`, `@payloadcms/ui` and `@payloadcms/richtext-lexical`; not for `next`, which takes none from there). Mutation: with the `@types` peers of `@payloadcms/ui` and `richtext-lexical` removed from `packageExtensions`, `check:workspace` exits 1 with `FAIL  apps/storefront → @payloadcms/ui: @types/react 18.3.31, expected 19.x` (and the same for `richtext-lexical`), and the old React-only check stayed green; restored, the lockfile is unchanged. (i) **`NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` is written into the build,** so `serverURL`, `cors` and `csrf` follow the value at `next build`, not at `next start`. Checked: built with `https://baked.example`, started with `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000`: a cookie-authenticated write with `Origin: http://localhost:8000` gave 403, with `Origin: https://baked.example` 201. `.env.example` says so; M7 builds with the production URL. The fallback stays, because the no-`.env` build must keep passing in CI. Checks after the fixes: storefront typecheck, lint, tests (**50**: the 39, plus 1 for `readVersions`, 6 for the admin guard, 3 for the locale filter and 1 for the language lists), root `typecheck`, `lint`, `test` (`Tasks: 8 / 9 / 3 successful`), `payload migrate` on an empty database (19 tables), `generate:types` and `generate:importmap` (no diff beyond the new role description, which is committed), `migrate:create --skip-empty` (no file), and root `pnpm build` (miss, then `FULL TURBO`). **Not changed, with the reason:** the `markets` global still has no uniqueness or cross-reference checks (duplicate country or language codes, a language with no `medusaLocale` row, a `primaryCountry` that is not a country): the plan gives T10 "the validation and the library". `withPayload`'s `Accept-CH` and `Critical-CH` headers still cover every route: the check recorded them and M7 decides where they apply. `src/migrations` stays outside `tsc` and ESLint: the generated imports break `verbatimModuleSyntax`, and a step that rewrites them is worth adding with the first hand-written migration. Payload's `payload-locked-documents` access is Payload's default (above).

## Markets library (T10)

**Result: every check in T10's Verify list passes. T11 can start.** `apps/storefront/src/lib/markets/` holds the pure functions of SPEC §8.5 and §12 (`resolve`, `buildPublicUrl`, `hreflangAlternates`, `toInternalKey`) and `getMarketMap()`; `lib/shell-markets.ts` and its 6 tests are gone, and the root layout reads the map. The 145 new tests in `lib/markets` pass on the seeded map, on a per-country domain, and on D17's Brazilian, Mongolian, US-only and Greek-only shops with no code change; 24 mutations were each caught. Beyond the Verify list, `getMarketMap()` was run against the real `payload` database in `next dev` and `next start`, with a map saved through the admin API, a map changed while the server ran, and an empty global. Three things differ from what the plan assumed: the layout can't read the map directly, because Cache Components refuse uncached I/O and `Date.now()` in a prerendered root layout (item 1); the development-only fallback also has to cover `next build`, because CI has no database (item 2); and public URLs have no trailing slash, so SPEC's `/us/` is notation for `/us` (item 3).

### Changes from the review of this PR

A code review of the diff made 14 points. Fixed in code, each with a test that fails without the fix:

1. **A read that never answers held every caller.** `createMarketSource` had no timeout and shares one read between callers, so a database host that goes silent (pg's default connect timeout is none) left every `get()` pending and the last good map was never served. The cause is in the pool, so `payload.config.ts` now sets `connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000` (checked against a blackholed address: `Connection terminated due to connection timeout` after the limit; `getPayload` caches a hung init attempt, so without a limit every later call would await the same one). A read also gives up after `MARKET_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), which covers a connection that goes silent after it was made, and counts as unreachable: the last good map keeps serving, and is kept for a further TTL. A read that timed out is still running, so the next caller joins it instead of starting another.
2. **`turbo run build` did not pass `PAYLOAD_*` to `next build`, nor hash them.** `apps/storefront/turbo.json` adds `"env": ["$TURBO_EXTENDS$", "PAYLOAD_*"]` to `build` (the root list is kept). `turbo run build --dry=json` lists `PAYLOAD_*` for `@shopblok/storefront#build` only, and setting `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` changes its hash. **Limit:** what the database holds can't be hashed, so a cached build replays the map it was built with until a request revalidates it (30 s).
3. **An unset or stale `primaryCountry` or `fallbackMarket` made the map unusable.** Both are optional in the admin, and `primaryCountryCode` and `fallbackMarketOf` already cope with them (first open country, primary market). `marketMapProblems` no longer reports them, so closing the country a fallback points at no longer freezes the shop on its previous map.
4. **The build fallback hid every read error.** While `next build` runs, an unreachable global now falls back to the fixtures only when `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` is unset (CI, no `.env`). With it set, a wrong password, a missing table or a bad secret fails the build instead of shipping the seeded markets. Checked: no `.env` → `Generating static pages (11/11)`, exit 0; `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL=postgres://u:p@127.0.0.1:1/none` → `Failed to collect page data for /[market]`, exit 1.
5. **Two markets with one `htmlLang` made the map unusable.** The override belongs to a language and applies to every country that uses it (the admin field says so now), so Brazil and Portugal both in `pt` with `pt-BR` is a normal setup. `marketMapProblems` no longer reports it; `hreflangAlternates` keeps the first market for a value.
6. **`buildPublicUrl` let `/\host` through.** Leading backslashes and whitespace are stripped along with slashes, as `resolve` already encoded `\` for its redirects.
7. **`getCurrentMarket` had no tests.** The pure part moved to `lib/market-locale.ts` (`withLocale`: English copy for a language `@shopblok/i18n` has no catalog for) with 3 tests, and `market.test.ts` has the key checks the T08 stand-in had (`cy`, `EL-CY`, empty).
8. **`resolve` and `hreflangAlternates` rebuilt the market list two or three times per call.** Each now builds it once and passes it to `primaryMarket(map, markets)` and `fallbackMarketOf(map, markets)` (the parameter is optional). No cache: a memo per map object would miss on the render path, where `use cache` hands back a fresh object, and would rest on maps never being mutated.
9. **A second hand-copied Medusa locale list.** `MEDUSA_LANGUAGES` in `payload/locales.ts` is derived from `MEDUSA_LOCALES` (same 36 languages, same order, so `LANGUAGES` and the `_locales` enum are unchanged), and the admin description counts the codes (`${MEDUSA_LOCALES.length}`).
10. **`clearMarketMapCache`, `source.clear()` and `ttlMs` were unused**, and `clear()` had two flaws for the day it is used (it dropped the last good map, and an in-flight read re-filled the cache with the pre-save map). Removed, so M4 writes the version it needs. The two flaws go with it.

**Not changed in code, with the reason:**

- **`revalidate: 30` gives every page under `[market]` a 30 s shell** and **`revalidateTag('global:markets')` alone would re-read the in-process map**. Both are the cost of having no invalidation hook yet; the 30 s is how an edit reaches the shell until M4 adds one. Written into `cached-market-map.ts`: the hook must also drop the in-process map in each server process, and only then can `revalidate` become hours.
- **A CMS page whose slug is an open country or language code** (`en`, `de`, `us`) is shadowed, so `buildPublicUrl` and `resolve` are not inverses for that path. That is the rule the plan chose ("wins over a CMS slug of the same name"). The `pages` collection should refuse such slugs; T13 (or the task that adds page URLs) has to check them against the open countries and languages.

Checks after the fixes: storefront tests **206** (was 194; 14 files), `typecheck` and `lint` clean, `next build` with no `.env` and no database exit 0 with all 7 `<html lang>` prerendered, and the build with an unreachable configured database exits 1.

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 5 are applied in this PR (plan text, code); 6 to 10 are notes for the tasks named; 11 lists limits that are known and not fixed.

1. **The render path must read the map through `use cache`; the proxy reads it directly.** The first version of the shell called `getMarketMap()` from the layout: `next build` failed with `Route "/[market]": Next.js encountered the unstable value Date.now() while prerendering` (the cache's TTL check). I did not try the I/O alone with a different clock: Next's migration guide says uncached data in a prerendered route must be cached with `use cache` or sit behind Suspense, and the root layout, which holds `<html lang>`, can't sit behind one. **Fix:** `getRenderMarketMap()` (`cached-market-map.ts`) is a `"use cache"` function with `cacheLife({ stale: 300, revalidate: 30, expire: 3600 })` and `cacheTag("global:markets")`, and `getCurrentMarket()` calls it. The numbers are chosen, not default: `cacheLife` leaves a read out of the prerender when `expire` is under 5 minutes or `stale` under 30 s, and the layout would then block; `revalidate: 30` keeps the shell about as fresh as the 30 s in-process cache beneath it (worst case roughly 60 s, which the live checks measured: 52 s in dev, under 70 s in `next start`); the tag is for the Payload hook that M4 adds (`revalidateTag('global:markets', …)`), which has no hook yet. The in-process cache's clock is `performance.now()` (monotonic, and the one clock Cache Components accept in a render). **The proxy (T11) has no render to prerender: it calls `getMarketMap()`** from `lib/markets/get-market-map.ts`, which has no `server-only` import so that a proxy bundle can take it (not tried; add it if the proxy build accepts it). This is S1 evidence too: **Payload's Local API works inside `use cache` in `next dev` and `next start`** (reads, a changed global shown without a restart); `revalidateTag` itself was not exercised.
2. **The fixtures stand in for the global in development and while `next build` runs, never in production, and "unusable" and "unreachable" differ.** The plan said "development only". CI's `check` job builds with no database (T16), and `next build` now renders the layout, so the build needs a map: while `NEXT_PHASE` is `phase-production-build`, an unusable global (empty, or incomplete: no enabled country, a country with no language, a language with no Medusa locale, …) gives the seeded map and a warning, and so does an unreachable one (no secret, no database) **when `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` is unset**, which is CI's case. A build that has a database configured and can't read it fails (review of this PR, item 4 below). In development only an unusable global does; an unreachable database is an error, because a developer who forgot `pnpm infra:up` should hear it. In production neither does: `MarketMapError` with a message that says what to do, HTTP 500 (checked). **Once a map has been read, a later empty, incomplete or unreachable global keeps the last good map serving, with a warning** (checked live), because the admin accepts a half-filled global and one bad save must not take the shop down. A production build made with no database prerenders the seeded keys from the fixtures; they are replaced after `revalidate` (30 s) if the real map differs, and a real map's keys render on request. So **builds that ship should have the database** (Vercel's build has the environment). `NEXT_PHASE` was confirmed by the warning appearing during `next build` only.
3. **Public URLs have no trailing slash.** SPEC §8.5 and §12 write `/us/`, `/ch/fr/`. `trailingSlash` is off (the default), so Next redirects `/us/` to `/us`; `buildPublicUrl` and `hreflangAlternates` give `/us`, `/ch/fr` and `/` for the primary market. `resolve` accepts both and keeps a slash on a redirect (`/cy/store/` → `/store/`). SPEC v0.6 (T17) should say the slash is notation. **For T11:** Next removes a trailing slash with a 308 before the proxy runs (not tried here), so `/cy/` may arrive as `/cy` after a redirect; check `curl -I` of `/cy/` and decide whether `skipTrailingSlashRedirect` is needed for the table's "301 to `/`".
4. **A market on its own domain is an absolute URL, and the main host still serves every market.** The plan says "on that domain the country segment is dropped" and nothing else. Decisions (mine, easy to change in `resolve.ts` and `url.ts`): on the domain, only that country is served (its default language at `/`, extra languages at `/{lang}/`; `/us` there is a CMS page of that country); `buildPublicUrl` and the hreflang list give `https://shop.ch/fr` for it, so links from other markets land on the domain; on any other host the country is still served under the usual segments (no redirect to the domain: it may not be wired to the Vercel project yet). Canonical tags (M-later) should point at the domain URL. The `host` argument of `resolve` is compared without scheme, port, case or trailing dot, so pass the `Host` header as it is.
5. **`medusaLocale` is validated against the 51 codes, not against the row's language.** D8 and the plan: the owner chooses. A Croatian shop (Medusa has no `hr` locale) can use `en-US`; a wrong choice for a language gets a hint (`For pt, use pt-BR or pt-PT.`). `marketMapProblems` (`global.ts`, pure) repeats the membership check for data that bypassed the admin. **Not added, as T09 left it:** uniqueness and cross-reference checks on save, because S3's tests (and T09's live checks) save partial globals on purpose (countries without a `languages` row, no `primaryCountry`); the library decides what a partial global means (item 2) instead of the admin refusing it.
6. **For T11** (also written into its section): the layout's job from step 3 is done by `getCurrentMarket()` (looks the key up in the map, `notFound()` before any Suspense); the not-found page's link (`/${market.key}`) and `global-not-found.tsx`'s `"/" as Route` are the two places to switch to `buildPublicUrl` once the proxy serves public URLs. `resolve` returns `{ kind: "rewrite", market, internalPath }` (`internalPath` is `""` for a market's home, else starts with `/`, so `new URL('/' + market.key + internalPath, req.url)` works as the plan has it), `{ kind: "redirect", status: 301, location }` (path and query on the same host; use 308 for non-GET if wanted) or `{ kind: "notFound" }`. **`next start` needs the `markets` global filled, because production has no fallback** (item 2): the recipe is in "Smaller observations" below until T13's bootstrap does it.
7. **For T13:** `bootstrap-payload.ts` seeds the global from `@shopblok/fixtures`: `mapFromGlobal` reads exactly the shape `findGlobal` returns, and the fixtures' `MarketMap` is that shape (`countries[].languages` as strings, `languages[]` with `code` and `medusaLocale`, `primaryCountry`, `fallbackMarket`); `marketMapProblems(fixtures)` is `[]`, which a test checks. Seed only `languages[]` rows for languages some country uses.
8. **For T14:** `doctor` can't read the global over REST with an `integrations` key (the read is staff-only, T09 item 5), so its "global is empty" warning needs a different read (SQL on the `payload` database, or `payload run`). The reasons are in `marketMapProblems` (storefront `lib/markets/global.ts`); `doctor` can't import the app, so it needs its own, shorter test (`select count(*) from markets_countries`).
9. **For S1:** see item 1 (Local API in `use cache`). The build prerendered `/[market]` as `◐` with `○` for each key, as before.
10. **For T12:** its check `grep -R "process.env" apps/storefront/src` (outside `lib/env.ts`, `payload.config.ts`, `src/scripts/`) already finds two files: `payload/collections/users.ts` (T09, `NODE_ENV`) and `lib/markets/get-market-map.ts` (T10, `defaultAllowFallback(process.env)`, which reads `NODE_ENV`, `NEXT_PHASE` and `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL`). `lib/env.ts` doesn't exist yet. T12 moves both behind it, or lists them in the check; `defaultAllowFallback` already takes the environment as an argument, so the second is a one-line change.
11. **Limits to know, not fixed:** (a) `htmlLang` belongs to a language, not to a market, so two countries that share a language share its `<html lang>` override; without an override each gets `{language}-{COUNTRY}`, and two markets that end up with the same value share one hreflang entry (the first in the map's order keeps it); the map is still served. (b) A single-country shop with an extra language (`/en/…`) that opens a second country gets `/cy/en/…` for it, as SPEC §8.5 has it, so SPEC's "adding a country never changes existing URLs" doesn't hold for that one case. (c) Country and language segments are matched in lowercase only.

All but one took "the default or recommended option the plan or NEXT-SESSION gives", which is what is already in the repo, so **no code or environment changed**. They are D18 to D23 in the plan (§1): the three Payload defaults stay as they are (D18); the two market lists stay by hand, no generator (D19); no root Prettier config, `format:check` or CI step in M0 (D20); the compose ports stay published on all interfaces (D21); `mailpit.axllent.org` is not added to the allowed domains, so the saved environment copy is not rebuilt (D22); and `apps/storefront/AGENTS.md` stays (D23, an explicit answer).

### Smaller observations

- **Saving the seeded map by hand** (until T13's `bootstrap` does it; needed for `next start`, which has no fallback). With the server on `localhost:8000` and a migrated database:
  ```bash
  B=http://localhost:8000
  curl -s -X POST $B/api/users/first-register -H 'content-type: application/json' -H "Origin: $B" \
    -d '{"email":"owner@example.com","password":"correct-horse-battery","confirm-password":"correct-horse-battery"}'
  TOKEN=$(curl -s -X POST $B/api/users/login -H 'content-type: application/json' -H "Origin: $B" \
    -d '{"email":"owner@example.com","password":"correct-horse-battery"}' | node -pe 'JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(0,"utf8")).token')
  curl -s -X POST $B/api/globals/markets -H 'content-type: application/json' -H "Authorization: JWT $TOKEN" -H "Origin: $B" -d '{
    "countries":[{"code":"cy","enabled":true,"languages":["el"]},{"code":"us","enabled":true,"languages":["en"]},
      {"code":"gb","enabled":true,"languages":["en"]},{"code":"de","enabled":true,"languages":["de"]},
      {"code":"fr","enabled":true,"languages":["fr"]},{"code":"ch","enabled":true,"languages":["de","fr"]}],
    "languages":[{"code":"en","medusaLocale":"en-US"},{"code":"el","medusaLocale":"el-GR"},
      {"code":"de","medusaLocale":"de-DE"},{"code":"fr","medusaLocale":"fr-FR"}],
    "primaryCountry":"cy","fallbackMarket":"en-us"}'
  ```
  The first call is `first-register` only while `users` is empty. A Brazil-only map is `{"countries":[{"code":"br","enabled":true,"languages":["pt"]}],"languages":[{"code":"pt","medusaLocale":"pt-BR"}],"primaryCountry":"br","fallbackMarket":"pt-br"}`. The map takes up to about a minute to reach the pages (two 30 s caches).
- **After a restart, `next start` answered a page from before it.** The build had prerendered `/pt-br` with the Brazil map; the map was then changed (so `/pt-br` became a 404) and the global emptied; after the restart `/pt-br` was still a 404 (not the 200 of the build's file, not the 500 of a fresh render). I did not look at why, and the `.next` of that run is gone. My guess is that Next writes a revalidated page back into `.next/server/app/` and serves that file after a restart; unverified. If T11's Playwright runs see pages from an earlier map, build fresh before a run that changes the map.
- **`pt-br` after the map no longer has it is Next's default 404** (`<html id="__next_error__">`), as T08 item 2 describes; the proxy never rewrites to a key it didn't choose.
- **First request in `next dev` 7.96 s** (compile and Payload start), then 0.09 to 0.25 s. In `next start` the first request after a start took 0.59 s with a map to read; warm 4 to 8 ms by `curl` (not T11's `performance.now()` measurement).
- **The mutation script** was a throwaway in the session's scratch directory; the 24 mutations are listed above and each took one `git checkout` to revert.
- The Greek, German and French copy is still the earlier sessions' and not checked by a native speaker; T10 added no copy.

## Proxy and the `[market]` root (T11)

**Result: every check in T11's Verify list passes. T12 can start.** `apps/storefront/src/proxy.ts` turns public market URLs into the internal `/{key}/…` routes (SPEC §8.1, §8.5): `/` is `<html lang="el-CY">`, `/us` `en-US`, `/gb`, `/de`, `/fr`, `/ch` `de-CH`, `/ch/fr` `fr-CH`; `/cy` is a 301 to `/`; the internal key (`/el-cy`) is a 404 from `global-not-found` in English; `/admin` and `/api/pages` are not rewritten. Two things differ from what the plan assumed. **The proxy reads the map from a route, not from Payload**: importing Payload into it cost 550 ms on the first request (limit 150 ms), so T11 used the plan's own fallback (item 1). **`/cy/` is two hops, not one**: Next's 308 for the trailing slash runs before the proxy, and I left `skipTrailingSlashRedirect` off (item 3).

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 4 are applied in this PR (plan text and D10, code); the rest are notes for the tasks named.

1. **The proxy reads the map from `/next/markets`, not from Payload (D10, T11's fallback).** The plan's criterion was a cold miss over 150 ms with Payload imported into the proxy; it was **549.6 ms**. So `proxy.ts` calls `getProxyMarketMap()` (`lib/markets/proxy-market-map.ts`): `createMarketSource` (the same TTL, timeout, last-good-map and fallback rules as T10) over `fetch(NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL + '/next/markets')`. The route (`app/next/markets/route.ts`, 8 lines) returns `getRenderMarketMap()` (the `use cache` entry, tag `global:markets`, `revalidate: 30`), is prerendered at build, and sits under `/next`, which the matcher skips. A `MarketMap` has the shape `mapFromGlobal` reads, so the proxy's source validates it again. **The fetch URL comes from the environment and never from the `Host` header**: with a forged `Host` the proxy would have fetched an attacker's host and cached its answer as the market map for every visitor. `getProxyMarketMap()` takes no request, so it can't; the tests pin the URL to the environment. `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` must therefore be a URL the server can reach itself (default `http://localhost:8000`; behind a proxy that is the public URL). **Freshness:** a saved map takes 30 to about 65 s to reach the proxy (measured: 30, 54 and 63 s), because the proxy's 30 s cache sits over the route's `revalidate: 30`, which sits over `getMarketMap()`'s 30 s. M4's `revalidateTag('global:markets')` hook refreshes the route and the pages but not the proxy's in-process cache, which still waits up to 30 s. **What it buys:** the proxy bundle has no Payload (no config, no pool, no 550 ms on a cold proxy function), and the warm path is unchanged (0.009 ms). **What it doesn't:** in one self-hosted process Payload still starts once, inside the route, and that start can land on a request (320 to 370 ms, item 8), so the cold cost is 57 ms at best and 370 ms at worst against 550 ms, and the 150 ms line is met only some of the time. D10 and the plan's T11 text say so now.
2. **A not found is a rewrite to `/`, not to `/_not-found` or a path of our own.** `global-not-found` renders for a URL that matches no route. `/_not-found` is one segment, so `[market]` takes it as a market key and the layout's `notFound()` renders Next's default page (`html#__next_error__`, no `lang`); I saw exactly that in `next dev` before changing it. A path such as `/_/x` works today and stops working when `[market]/[...slug]` exists. `/` matches nothing now or later (no `app/page.tsx`, every route sits under `[market]`). `NOT_FOUND_PATH` in `proxy-response.ts` says why; a unit test pins the value.
3. **`skipTrailingSlashRedirect` stays off, so `/cy/` is two hops (308, then 301).** `curl -I localhost:8000/cy/` → `308 Location: /cy` (Next, before the proxy), then `/cy` → `301 /`. With the flag on, `/cy/` would reach `resolve` and be one 301, but the flag also stops Next normalising `/admin/`, `/api/pages/` and every other route (both would answer 200 at the two URLs), the proxy would have to redo the slash rule for the storefront (308 for `/us/`, a slashless target for each redirect), and the docs call it a flag "most projects don't need". The cost of leaving it off is one extra hop on a URL no link of ours produces. To change it: set the flag in `next.config.ts`, strip the slash in `respondToMarketRequest` for a rewrite and for each redirect location, and change the `/cy/` test. The Verify table's `/us/` etc. end on the slashless URL in the browser.
4. **The rewrite keeps the query string, which `new URL('/' + key + path, req.url)` (the plan's and the prompt's text) does not.** `new URL(path, base)` drops the base's search. `respondToMarketRequest` clones `req.nextUrl` and sets `pathname`; tests pin `?page=2&sort=asc` through a rewrite and through a redirect.
5. **`next/experimental/testing/server` exports `unstable_doesMiddlewareMatch`, not the `unstable_doesProxyMatch` the proxy page documents.** `src/proxy.test.ts` uses the real name. It can't import `proxy.ts` (Vitest has no `@payload-config`, and Next reads `config.matcher` as a literal), so it reads the literal out of the file's source and fails if the file stops having one. 32 URLs, both ways.
6. **The matcher leaves a file to Next, and Next collapses `//` itself.** A file is a last segment that ends in `.` and a letter and then letters and digits (`logo.png`, `robots.txt`), plus `/.well-known/`; the first version skipped any dot anywhere, which sent `/products/1.5l-bottle` and `/ch/fr/store/size-8.5` to `[market]` as if `products` were a market (changed in the review, below). So `/cy/evil.example` is a 404 that never reaches `resolve`, and `/cy//x` arrives as `/cy/x` after a 308. A first segment of `admin`, `api` or `next` is reserved for Payload and the draft-mode routes: a CMS page with one of those slugs is not reachable at the primary country's `/…` URL.
7. **`x-middleware-rewrite` is visible on responses in `next start`** (`/` shows `x-middleware-rewrite: /el-cy`). The spec uses its presence and absence to prove "rewritten" and "not rewritten". It tells a visitor the internal key; M7 (headers) can strip it if that matters.
8. **Limits to know, not fixed.** (a) **The first request after a restart can take 320 to 370 ms**: the first hits of `/next/markets` (323 ms and 366 ms by `curl`, then 10 ms), and the first TTL refresh in a process (372 ms), include Payload starting inside the route while the proxy waits. In another run the first read took 57 ms. I did not isolate the difference (build age, or whether a page was rendered first). The `cacheLife` docs describe stale-while-revalidate, which is not what I measured; S1 lists Next issue #99741 (cache lost after `next start` restarts), which may be it. I did not look further. An `instrumentation.ts` that starts Payload at boot would hide it on a self-hosted server but would also start Payload in every serverless function (the proxy's included), so I did not add it. (b) **An empty or unreadable global in production no longer answers 500 on a running or freshly restarted server.** T10 said a fresh process over an empty global answers 500; now the route's last good entry (the build's copy) keeps serving for up to its `expire` (1 h) and the log shows `MarketMapError` on each regeneration. After `expire` it is a 500. A build made over an empty global ships the seeded fixtures in that copy (the build logs it). (c) The route is public. It answers with the open countries only (`withoutClosedCountries`), which is what the hreflang tags print; a country with `enabled: false` and its domain is not published (changed in the review, below). (d) On Vercel preview deployments, deployment protection blocks the proxy's fetch of its own route unless the URL is the production one or a bypass is configured: M7's hosting work.
9. **For T12:** its `process.env` grep (`apps/storefront/src` outside `lib/env.ts`, `payload.config.ts`, `src/scripts/`) now finds three files, not two: `payload/collections/users.ts`, `lib/markets/get-market-map.ts` and `lib/markets/proxy-market-map.ts` (`process.env` as the default `env`; reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, `NODE_ENV`, `NEXT_PHASE`, `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL`). `createProxyMarketMap({ env })` already takes the environment as an argument, so moving it behind `lib/env.ts` is a one-line change. `proxy.ts` imports no Payload, so `lib/env.ts` may be imported there.
10. **For T13:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` in the storefront `.env` is now also the address the proxy fetches (item 1); `.env.example` says so. Production still needs the `markets` global filled (T10): bootstrap's seed of it is what makes the first `next start` work.
11. **For T16:** the `markets` suite runs against `next start` with the global filled and the build made after it (`playwright.config.ts` starts nothing, says why, and `beforeAll` fails with that hint). `PORT` 8000 and `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` `http://localhost:8000` are the defaults, so CI needs no variable. The `wiring` and `payload-admin` suites are T12's and S1's.
12. **For S1:** more evidence, none of it a verdict. A `'use cache'` function reading the Local API is prerendered into a **route handler** too (`○ /next/markets`, `30s`, `1h`) and refreshes while the server runs; the admin and `/api/*` work through a proxy whose matcher skips them; `/admin` login and logout pass with `cacheComponents` and `partialPrefetching` on, in `next start`. `revalidateTag` still wasn't exercised (no hook).
13. **For M4:** the Payload hook that revalidates `global:markets` also has to make the proxy's in-process cache drop its map, or accept 30 s. Today the chain is: proxy 30 s → route `revalidate: 30` → `getMarketMap()` 30 s (item 1).

### Changes from the review of this PR (T11)

A code review of the diff made 14 points. Fixed (unit tests 308, from 288; typecheck, lint and Prettier clean; `next build` passes; Playwright against that build with no `.env` and no database: 25 of 28 pass, the 3 that need Payload's secret and database (`/api/pages`, `/api/globals/markets`, `/admin`) were not run where they could pass):

- **The proxy never waits for a refresh once it has a map** (`staleWhileRevalidate` in `createMarketSource`, set by `createProxyMarketMap`). Before, every request that arrived after the 30 s TTL waited for the one read, up to the 10 s timeout, and a failed read restarted the TTL, so a slow route stalled the shop for 10 s in every 30. Now the old map answers at once and the read runs in the background; only a first read blocks. `getMarketMap()` keeps the old rule (its callers sit under `use cache`, which already serves stale). In a serverless function the background read may pause with the instance; the next call joins it or starts another. The 30 to about 65 s above holds under steady traffic: the first request after an idle spell gets the map the instance last held, and renews it for the next.
- **The proxy checks the shape of the reply.** `mapFromGlobal` ran on whatever came back, outside the guard in `read()`, so another app answering 200 on `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` (a string, `{"countries":{}}`) threw a `TypeError` past the last-good-map rule and every request got a 500. The loader now requires an object with a `countries` and a `languages` list (the route always sends both) and otherwise throws `<url> did not answer a market map; is NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL this app?`, which the source handles like any failed read: the last good map keeps serving, with a warning. A reply that is not JSON gets the same message.
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` is read as a literal** (`process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`), like `payload.config.ts`, so `next build` writes it into the bundle as `.env.example` says; the proxy had read it through the `process.env` object, which is not replaced. An address that is not a URL, an unreachable one and a bad status now name the URL they used. The `localhost:8000` default stays (CI and `next start` rely on it).
- **`/next/markets` answers with the open countries only.** It returned closed countries, their domains and languages to anyone, while `/api/globals/markets` is staff-only.
- **`global-not-found` stays a 404 when the map cannot be read** (an empty global, a database that is down, no cached entry): `homeHref()` logs the error and the page renders without its link instead of a 500.
- **The matcher skips a file, not a dot** (item 6 above): `/products/1.5l-bottle` reaches the proxy. `proxy.test.ts` has 4 new storefront paths and 2 new skipped ones.
- **Tests:** `proxy.test.ts` imports `config` instead of parsing the source of `proxy.ts` (it imports no Payload now) and tests `proxy()` itself; the timeout test checks `AbortSignal.timeout(MARKET_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS)`, not only that a signal was passed.
- **Comments:** `current-market.ts` and `cached-market-map.ts` said the proxy and the render path read one map and that the proxy calls `getMarketMap()`; neither is true.

**Not changed, with the reason:**

- **The build's copy of the route in production (8b).** It is the same as every prerendered page: they all ship the build's map until they regenerate, and `defaultAllowFallback` only decides what a *build* may use. Making the route dynamic would lose the cheap first read that D10 bought. Unchanged and documented in 8(b).
- **Four 30 s caches in a row, and a second cold start in a serverless route.** This is the design of D10's fallback, with its measured 30 to about 65 s; a lighter source or one invalidation path is M4's and M7's decision, in "For you to decide" of the PR.
- **The 404 page links to the primary market, not the visitor's.** Until `[market]/[...slug]` exists (T12), `/us/does-not-exist` reaches `global-not-found`, which has no market. Carrying the market through the rewrite needs a request header and makes the page dynamic; the catch-all makes it unnecessary.
- **A first segment of `admin`, `api` or `next` is not a storefront path.** It is Payload's and the draft-mode routes', and the tests pin it (item 6).
- **`getRenderMarketMap()` is read twice in `[market]/not-found.tsx`.** Both reads are the same `use cache` entry; returning the map from `getCurrentMarket()` would add an API for no saved work.

### Smaller observations

- **Playwright's `page.request` did not send the `Secure` `payload-token` cookie to `http://localhost:8000`** (production mode sets `Secure`), so `me` came back with no user right after a successful UI login; reading it with `fetch` inside the page worked. Use the page, not `page.request`, for authenticated calls in the admin suites (S1, T16), or log in through the API and pass the JWT header as T09 did.
- **`next dev` first requests:** `GET /api/nothing-here 404 in 6.4s` (compile and Payload start), `/admin` 4.8 s, then 0.07 to 0.3 s. In dev the proxy also logs its time (`proxy.ts: 4ms`).
- **The market `not-found.tsx` renders its own page only in a browser.** `curl` of a `notFound()` under `[market]` shows `html#__next_error__` in `next dev` (the client swaps in the not-found boundary), and Chromium shows the market's page. T08 saw the same.
- **`next dev` for the probe needed `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8001`**: the proxy fetches that URL, and the default points at 8000, where another server answered. Two servers on two ports share `.next` only in separate subfolders (`.next/dev` and `.next`), which worked.
- **`pnpm add` refused nothing:** pnpm's one-day `minimumReleaseAge` was met (1.64.0 is two days old). Playwright's browsers are in `/opt/ms-playwright` (`chromium-1248`); nothing was downloaded.
- **Measurement scripts** (`latency.mjs`, `refresh.mjs`, `mutate.py`, the admin and not-found Chromium scripts) were throwaways in the session's scratch directory; the numbers above are from them.
- The Greek, German and French copy of the not-found pages is still the earlier sessions' and not checked by a native speaker.

## Medusa DAL and the wiring page (T12)

**Result: every check in T12's Verify list passes except the "5xx" in the fourth, which cannot hold in this design; T13 can start.** `apps/storefront/src/lib/medusa/` (SDK, regions, products), `lib/cms/site.ts`, `lib/cache.ts` and `lib/env.ts` are the data layer, and the market home (`[market]/page.tsx`) is the wiring page: the market, its language, the region and its currency, the store's price-filter currencies, Payload's published pages, and 4 products with titles in the market's language and prices through `formatMoney`. `/` shows Greek titles and `12,00 €` (`el-CY`), `/us` English and `$14.00`, `/ch/fr` French and `12.00 CHF`, and `/br` (a market opened in the admin after the build) Brazilian prices as `R$ 366,00`, with no storefront edit. **With the backend stopped the page shows its error state and the server stays up, but the HTTP status is 200, not 5xx:** the data is read in a streamed part of the page, and Next fixes the status when the first byte leaves (item 2). Three other things differ from the plan's text: the wrapper is `cached(fn, { name, tags, life })`, because Next refuses the closure form (item 1); `@medusajs/types` is not a dependency of the storefront, because adding it broke the backend's build (item 4); and `medusaEnv()` is async, so the proxy does not carry zod (item 5).
