### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 5 are applied in this PR (plan text, code); the rest are notes for the tasks named.

1. **`cached(fn, opts)` is `cached(fn, { name, tags, life })`.** The plan's wrapper (`return async (...args) => { "use cache"; cacheTag(…); cacheLife(…); return fn(...args) }`) is refused by Next when the wrapper is created, because a variable the function closes over becomes part of the cache key and a function cannot be one (`Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components…`, observed in `next dev`). `lib/cache.ts` has one `"use cache"` function, `run(name, tags, life, args)`, which takes plain data: the name, the tags, the life and the wrapped function's own arguments are all in the key, and it looks the function up by name in a module-level map that `cached()` fills. So **`name` is required and must be unique**: every wrapped function shares `run`, whose key would otherwise be its location and its arguments only (two functions with the same arguments would share an entry). Arguments and results must be serializable. S1's fallback (`cacheComponents: false`) swaps `run` for `unstable_cache(load, [name], { tags, revalidate })` and nothing else changes. Checked live: entries are keyed by name and arguments, errors are not cached, `revalidateTag` reaches them (above). The Next docs advise against putting `cacheLife` in a shared helper ("so the cache behavior remains explicit"); this is the one place, on purpose.
2. **A 5xx on a down backend is not possible for a streamed part of a `◐` page, and the Verify text should say so.** Next sends the static shell first and fixes the status at `200`; an error in a part that streams in afterwards renders the error boundary (`$!`, a client render in the browser) and cannot change the status. The docs say so ("Streaming page responses begin with a `200` status code … Run the corresponding check before the response body streams", `loading` → Status codes). I measured the alternative the plan implies, a page that blocks: no `Suspense`, `await connection()` and `export const instant = false` (the documented opt-out). The build marks `/[market]` `ƒ`, the response is **still 200**, and the body is Next's own `<html id="__next_error__">`, not `error.tsx` (the resumed render has no boundary to recover in). So that variant is worse in every respect and was dropped. What is left: the page streams its data (`◐`, build needs no backend), the error state shows in the market's language, the cause is in the server log (the digest on the page matches), and nothing is cached. **For the owner, to decide when M1 brings the first real catalog page** (not blocking M0): (a) keep catalog data in `Suspense` parts like this one; (b) put it in the static shell with `use cache`, as SPEC §8.1 says for the home, PDP and PLP, which survives a backend outage (the shell is served stale) and gives a 5xx only for a shell rendered on request, but needs a build-time stand-in for CI builds that have no backend, like D10's fallback for the `markets` global (an unreachable backend fails a build that has `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL` set; one without it builds with an empty catalog); (c) a status route for monitors, for example `/next/status` answering 503 when Medusa or Payload does not answer. **Default: leave it as it is.**
3. **The wiring page reads Medusa and Payload in a request-time part of the page.** The market name and language are in the static shell (`◐`); the region, currencies, pages count and products are in a `<Suspense>` part behind `await connection()`, so `next build` calls neither Medusa nor Payload's database (CI has neither) and the DAL's `use cache` entries are filled at run time (in memory on a self-hosted server; on a serverless platform they mostly do not persist between requests, which is Next's documented default). Without `connection()` the build would call Medusa and fail with no backend.
4. **Do not add `@medusajs/types` to the storefront; it broke `medusa build`.** The first install added it (the plan's BS/SBP text says to use its types). `@medusajs/types` has an optional peer on `vite`; the storefront's `vite` is 8.3.3 and the backend's 7.3.7, so pnpm made a second copy of the package and pointed the hoisted one (`node_modules/.pnpm/node_modules/@medusajs/types`, which Medusa's own packages fall back on) at it. The backend's TypeScript then saw both copies in one program (170 imports resolved to one, 22 to the other) and `medusa build` failed with `TS2769`, `TS2322` and `TS2345` in `medusa-config.ts` and `src/search/product.ts`, files nobody had touched. Reproduced and fixed by dropping the dependency (the DAL imports no type from it; it infers them from the SDK, which depends on it), confirmed after `rm -rf node_modules` and a frozen install: one copy, the backend builds. **For a task that does need `HttpTypes`:** derive it (`Awaited<ReturnType<typeof sdk.store.product.list>>["products"][number]`), or make both apps resolve `vite` alike first; a `.pnpmfile.cjs` that drops `vite` from `@medusajs/types`'s peers would also do (not tried).
5. **`medusaEnv()` is async, and `lib/env.ts` imports zod itself.** The proxy imports `env` (it needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`), and a static `import { z } from "zod"` put zod into its bundle (248 KB → 353 KB, about 45 ms of start-up in Node); a schema built inside a function was not enough (zod runs code when it is imported). `lib/env.ts` is still the one reader of `process.env` and still zod-validated; the check just loads zod when it runs. `env` is a set of getters, each a literal `process.env.NAME`, because `next build` replaces `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` only in that exact form; `{ ...process.env }` finds nothing in a bundle. Parsing is lazy: importing the file reads nothing, so a build with no `.env` works. `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL` defaults to `http://localhost:9000` outside production and is **required in production**; the key must start `pk_` (the `apk_…` id is a common mistake); every problem is reported at once with the remedy.
6. **Regions are one cached list, and the currencies come from it.** `listRegions()` (tag `medusa:regions`, `minutes`: a newly opened market must not show "no region" for an hour) pages through `GET /store/regions` once, whatever the number of markets, and takes no locale (regions are the same in every language); `getRegionForCountry(country)` looks in it, `getSearchPriceCurrencies()` is its distinct, sorted currency codes: the storefront's copy of the backend's `SEARCH_PRICE_CURRENCIES` (D17), read, never typed. The plan's "`getRegionForCountry`: tag `medusa:regions`" would have made one backend call per country. The wiring page shows that set as a row, so the D17 behaviour can be seen: the live Brazil check added `BRL` to it with no storefront edit. `listProducts({ regionId, locale, limit })` has tag `medusa:products` and `minutes`; the lifetimes are choices, nothing invalidates them yet.
7. **The DAL returns small DTOs, not the SDK's types:** `Region { id, name, currencyCode, countries[] }`, `ProductSummary { id, handle, title, thumbnail, price: Money | null }` with the lowest `calculated_amount` over the variants. The plan said fields include `*variants.calculated_price`; they do (`id,handle,title,thumbnail,*variants.calculated_price`), the entry cached is the DTO. `Money` is in major units, never ÷100 (tested).
8. **For T13:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_MEDUSA_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is a `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` value, so **`next build` writes it into the bundle**: a key written after a build (or a database that was wiped, which makes a new key) needs a new build. `pnpm bootstrap` must write the key before anything builds or starts the storefront, and the markets global before `next start`. `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL` is a plain server variable. Both are in `apps/storefront/.env.example` already. Bootstrap's `✓ done` for the key can check the format (`pk_…`), as `medusaEnv()` does.
9. **For T14:** `doctor` can check the key against `GET /store/regions` (HTTP 400 `Publishable API key required` if wrong or missing, T07) and that a region exists for every open country of the `markets` global; a market with no region renders the "no region" message, not an error, so nothing else warns about it.
10. **For T16:** the wiring suite can rely on `data-testid` `market`, `language`, `region`, `currency`, `price-currencies`, `pages`, `product` (4), `no-region`, `no-products` and `error-state` (the last one appears after hydration, with status 200). Prices are `formatMoney(amount, currency, htmlLang)`, so the expected text depends on the market's `htmlLang`. Kill the old `next start` before a rebuild, or the suite reads a half-deleted `.next`. In production `page.request` still does not send the `Secure` cookie (T11).
11. **For S1:** more evidence, still no verdict. Local API reads and the Medusa SDK work inside `use cache` in `next dev` and `next start`; `revalidateTag(tag, { expire: 0 })` from a route handler gives fresh content on the next request, `'max'` within the first (both through `cached()`); a failed `use cache` call caches nothing and recovers when the backend returns; the build marks `◐` with `cacheComponents` and `partialPrefetching` on. Not run: the draft-isolation load test, the admin suite, the `partialPrefetching` and W1 columns.
12. **Limits to know, not fixed.** (a) The status of a failed streamed part is 200 (item 2). (b) In the browser the failure also logs `Minified React error #441` (React's note that the server could not finish the part); the error state does not show it. (c) Product order follows creation order and changes after a database reset, so "the first 4" are not always the same 4. (d) The SDK sends `x-medusa-locale:` empty when no locale is given (the region call), which Medusa ignores. (e) The Greek, German and French copy of the new wiring strings is mine and not checked by a native speaker.

### Smaller observations

- **`docs.medusajs.com/resources/references/js-sdk/store/region` is an empty shell without JavaScript;** the same address ending in `.md` returns the page (13 KB for `region`). The environment allows both.
- **Vitest and the SDK:** Vite transforms the SDK's ESM build and prints hundreds of `Sourcemap … points to missing source files` lines; `vitest.config.ts` externalises it (`server.deps.external`). `server-only` is aliased to an empty module for the same kind of reason: it throws outside Next's server bundle.
- **`vi.resetModules()` + `await import()`** gives a fresh SDK and a fresh `use cache` registry per test; the tests stub `fetch`, not the SDK, so they see the headers and the URL the real SDK builds.
- **A foreground `sleep` is blocked** in this environment; waits are `until` loops that poll the page (the pages count took 32 s to follow a new page, the Brazil map 36 s and 56 s).
- **`pgrep -f 'pnpm start'` matched the shell that ran it** and killed it (exit 144); stop servers by PID from `ps`, as the prompt says.
- **Throwaways** (the Chromium reader `check.mjs`, `fill-markets.sh`, `mutate.py`, the two probe routes) were in the session's scratch directory or deleted; the numbers above are from them. The mutation list is in the "Mutations" row.
- **Commit trailers:** this session's commits say `Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>` without a model name, because the instructions forbid model identifiers in pushed artifacts; the earlier ones say `Claude Sonnet 5.5`.

## `pnpm bootstrap` (T13)

**Result: every check in T13's Verify list passes. T14 can start.** `pnpm bootstrap` takes a clone with nothing but a running compose stack to a working setup in **41 s** (limit: 5 minutes): `.env` files with generated secrets, both databases migrated, the seeds, the Medusa admin, the publishable key, the Payload admin and `integrations` user, and the `markets` global. A second run changes nothing (**0 done, 9 unchanged**; both `.env` files byte for byte the same, the row counts of all 170 tables the same). Its output is checked against the real result: the storefront it built from the written `.env` serves `/` in Greek with `49,00 €`, `/us` with `$54.00` and `/ch/fr` with `48.00 CHF`, and the Playwright `markets` suite passes (28). Seven things differ from the plan's text or are worth knowing (items 1 to 7 under "What should change in the plan"); the first is a hang this task found in its own first design.

### What `pnpm bootstrap` does

`node scripts/bootstrap.ts [--ci] [--no-demo] [--verbose]`, from the repo root. Every step ends as `✓ done`, `= unchanged` or `↷ skipped`, and stops the run at the first failure.

| Step | What it does | Counts as unchanged when |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Preflight | Node and pnpm against `engines`; each service reachable on the port the apps are configured for (Postgres by the protocol's SSLRequest, Redis by `PING`/`PONG`, Meilisearch by `/health`, Mailpit by TCP); and `docker compose port` must publish that same host port (D15) | always (it writes nothing) |
| 2 Env files | Creates `apps/backend/.env` and `apps/storefront/.env` from their examples; fills `JWT_SECRET`, `COOKIE_SECRET`, `PAYLOAD_SECRET`, `PREVIEW_SECRET`, `CRON_SECRET` and the shared `REVALIDATE_SECRET`. Never overwrites a value | nothing to create or fill |
| 3 Medusa migrations | `medusa db:migrate --execute-safe-links --execute-safe-search` | the output has no `● Migrating`, link table, `Search indexes migrated` or `Running migration script` line |
| 4 Medusa seeds | `seed-markets.ts`, then `seed-demo-catalog.ts` (`… skip` with `--no-demo`); each must print `Finished executing script.` | neither seed logged `Created`, `Updated`, `Linked`, `Filled` or non-zero `Translations: created/updated` |
| 5 Medusa admin | `medusa user --email=admin@example.com --password=<generated>` (the `=` form: a generated password can start with `-`, which the CLI would read as a flag after `-p`); on success writes `DEV_ADMIN_EMAIL` and `DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD` to the backend `.env` | "already exists" |
| 6 Publishable key | `print-publishable-key.ts`, then `NEXT_PUBLIC_MEDUSA_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` into the storefront `.env` (the one value bootstrap replaces, item 3) | the same key is there |
| 7 Payload migrations | `payload migrate` | no `Migrating:` line |
| 8 Payload users and markets | `payload run src/scripts/bootstrap-payload.ts`: the first admin while `users` is empty; the `integrations` user "Medusa sync" with the key from the backend `.env`; the `markets` global from `@shopblok/fixtures` while it has no country | all three already in place |
| 9 Payload key | `PAYLOAD_API_KEY` and `PAYLOAD_SERVER_URL` into the backend `.env` | already there |
| 10 | Medusa → Payload sync, Payload content seed | `↷ skipped (arrives in M4)` |
| 11 Doctor | `pnpm run doctor` | always (it writes nothing). Until T14 this was `↷ skipped (arrives in T14)`; now a doctor error fails the run and a warning does not |

`--ci` closes stdin for every command (this is always so), sets `CI=true` for them and prints no live status line; `--verbose` streams each command's output. Exit codes: 0 done, 1 a step failed, 2 a bad option. A failed step prints its cause, what to try, and the last 25 lines of the command's output with keys and passwords hidden.

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 7 are applied in this PR (code, plan text); 8 to 10 are notes for the tasks named.

1. **A command that asks a question must be stopped, because the answer can never come (found by the first version).** Every command runs with stdin closed, which stops Medusa's and Payload's prompts from waiting on a person, but a prompt library then waits on a stream that never ends: `payload migrate` after Payload's dev push (`PAYLOAD_DB_PUSH=true`, or the `dev` row in `payload_migrations`) asks `Would you like to proceed? › (y/N)` and bootstrap hung until killed. `run()` (`scripts/lib/exec.ts`) now ends a command whose output contains a `(y/N)`, `[Y/n]` or `(yes/no)` hint, in two chunks or with colour codes too, kills its whole process group, and returns the question; step 7 turns it into the remedy (`pnpm infra:reset`, or answer it yourself). `--force-accept-warning` is deliberately not passed: it would run the saved migrations on top of a schema the dev push already made. The plan only said "`pnpm bootstrap --ci` runs without prompts"; this is what makes it true for a command that prompts anyway.
2. **"Every step `= unchanged`" means no `✓ done`.** Steps 10 and 11 stay `↷ skipped` on every run, and the preflight and (later) the doctor write nothing, so they report `= unchanged`. Verify's second bullet is met when `0 done` and the `.env` files and rows are the same.
3. **Bootstrap replaces the publishable key when it differs, the one value it overwrites.** The plan said "never overwrite an existing value" for the secrets (step 2), which holds (a key in `.env` is kept; a blank one is filled). The key is different: it belongs to the database, a wiped database has a new one, and a stale key in `.env` makes every storefront request fail with a 400. `docker compose down -v && pnpm bootstrap` therefore ends `✓ done (replaced the key of an earlier database)`, and the step says that `next build` writes the key into the storefront, so it must build again (or `next dev` restart). Everything else that depends on the database is reused from `.env`: the admin's `DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and the integration's `PAYLOAD_API_KEY` come back identical on a new database.
4. **The Payload API key lives only in the backend `.env`, because Payload cannot give it back.** The plan said step 8 creates the `integrations` user "with a generated API key" and step 9 writes it. Payload stores a hash (`apiKeyIndex`) and an encrypted `apiKey`, and `omitAPIKey` removes both from every read, Local API included. So bootstrap decides the key first (the `PAYLOAD_API_KEY` in the backend `.env`, else a new UUID), passes it to `bootstrap-payload.ts` in `BOOTSTRAP_PAYLOAD_API_KEY`, and the script checks "this key works" the only honest way, `payload.auth({ headers: { Authorization: 'integrations API-Key <key>' } })`: it owns an `integrations` user → unchanged; otherwise the "Medusa sync" user is created, or given the key again (`rekeyed`, after a wiped database or a new `PAYLOAD_SECRET`). The key is never printed. Step 9 then writes it, which is a no-op when step 8 started from the `.env` value.
5. **The Payload admin and the Medusa admin share `DEV_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, with one gap handled.** The email is `admin@example.com` (the plan's; T07's session used `admin@shopblok.local`). If the Medusa admin exists and `DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is not in `.env` (a deleted `.env` over a kept database), bootstrap cannot know its password and writes none; if the Payload `users` table is also empty, it creates that admin with a generated password and prints it once, in the step's note. This pairing was not exercised live and has no test: it needs a database with a Medusa admin and an empty Payload `users` table, which no bootstrap run produces.
6. **`extractPublishableKey` is not imported from the backend.** The plan said T13 could import `apps/backend/src/scripts/lib/publishable-key.ts`. `tsc` in `scripts/` refuses it (`TS1287`: the backend's `package.json` has no `"type": "module"`, so the file is CommonJS to TypeScript while it has `export`). `extractMarker(output, "publishableKey")` (`scripts/lib/output.ts`) reads the same `{"publishableKey":…}` line, the Payload step uses it for `{"bootstrapPayload":…}`, and step 6 checks the `pk_` prefix. The format is now written in two places (the backend's printer and this reader); a change to the key's line has to touch both.
7. **`--no-demo` runs the demo seed with `skip`, and the seed decides.** As the plan says; it also means a production bootstrap (`NODE_ENV=production`) must pass `--no-demo`, because the seed refuses to run there (D7) and bootstrap reports that as a failure rather than hiding it.
8. **For T14:** `scripts/lib/checks.ts` already has what `doctor`'s probes need: `targetsFrom(env)` (hosts and ports from the env files' URLs, compose defaults for what is missing, no Redis when `REDIS_URL` is blank), `canConnect`, `redisAnswers` (a `PING`, because "established" log lines prove nothing, T06 item 5), `postgresAnswers` (the protocol's SSLRequest, no login), `meilisearchAnswers` (`/health`), and `observeCompose`/`judge`/`dockerUnavailableWarning` for the D15 ownership check. `scripts/lib/env-file.ts` has `getEnvValue` for reading both apps' env files. Medusa's `/health` and the storefront `/` need `fetch`. The `markets` global cannot be read over REST with an `integrations` key (T10 item 8); `payload run` with a script like `bootstrap-payload.ts` does it, or SQL on the `payload` database. The key check T12 item 9 proposed works: `GET /store/regions` with the key from the storefront `.env` returned the regions, and `GET /api/integrations/me` with the backend `.env`'s `PAYLOAD_API_KEY` returned the "Medusa sync" user (the `--online` check).
9. **For T14 (D17):** bootstrap does not compare `config/markets.ts` with the storefront's map and never will; that is `doctor`'s job (D19). It makes the backend half right, in the right order: the Brazil check shows regions, locales and a new search index version following one edit, in both directions (adding and removing a currency each plan a new index version).
10. **For T16 (CI):** `pnpm bootstrap --ci` is the call. On a runner it finds the compose stack (preflight is satisfied by `docker compose up -d --wait`), needs no `.env` (it creates them) and takes about 40 s plus the install. The `e2e-smoke` job can build the storefront right after it, because the key and the `markets` global are in place by then.

### Smaller observations

- **`medusa db:migrate`'s output, as it is parsed.** A first run holds 187 `● Migrating …` lines, `Syncing links…` with a `Created following links tables` box, `Migrating search indexes…` / `Search indexes migrated. They are filled when the application starts` and five `Running migration script` lines. A second run: 0 `● Migrating`, 39 `Skipped. Database is up-to-date for module.`, `Search indexes already up-to-date`, and `create-super-admin-role.js` listed as pending but not run (T07 item 6), which is why "pending" alone is not a change. The quoted lines in `output.test.ts` are from these runs.
- **`medusa user` for an existing user prints the error object and exits 1** (`message: 'User with email: admin@example.com, already exists.'`); `classifyUser` keys on that sentence, as T07 said.
- **`medusa exec` takes 4 to 5 s a script** (it boots the app), `medusa db:migrate` 12 to 17 s, `payload migrate` and `payload run` 3 to 4 s. Bootstrap boots Medusa five times (migrate, two seeds, user, key); a combined script would save about 10 s and was not worth coupling them.
- **The seeds' warnings** (`Region "Europe" differs from the seed …`) are shown as `!` notes under the step; Medusa's own `http.jwtSecret not found` warning is not, because bootstrap fills the secrets and a plain copy would repeat it on every line.
- **Windows:** `exec.ts` builds the whole command line for `pnpm` as one string for `cmd.exe` and quotes each argument (`quoteForCmd`, tested for both platforms from Linux); `docker` starts directly; paths are `node:path`; `chmod 600` on the `.env` files is skipped silently. None of it ran on Windows: that is W2. Children run in their own process group so that a timeout can stop the whole `pnpm → sh → node` chain, so a Ctrl-C in the terminal no longer reaches them and `exec.ts` stops them itself on `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` (on Windows with `taskkill /t /f`). A `SIGTERM` (from `timeout 120`) left no process behind; `SIGINT` uses the same handler and was not tried by hand.
- **`.env` files are written with mode 600** where the platform has modes (they hold the signing secrets and `DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD`).
- **`DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is a plain-text development password in a git-ignored file** and `medusa user -p` takes it as a command-line argument, so it is visible in `ps` for the 4 seconds that command runs. It is a throwaway account on a local stack; a shared host should not run bootstrap with real credentials.
- **No new dependency beyond `vitest` and `vite` for `scripts/`**, so the OSV check (T03's, for new packages) had nothing to look up: both are the versions the other packages already use.
- **Throwaways** (the mutation harness `mutate.py`, the shim `docker`, the row-count and `md5sum` helpers) were in the session's scratch directory; the numbers above are from them.

## `pnpm run doctor` (T14)

**Result: every check in T14's Verify list passes. T15 can start.** `pnpm run doctor` (never `pnpm doctor`, which is pnpm's own command) reports which of SPEC §10's seven integrations are live, probes the stack and both servers, and compares the backend's `config/markets.ts` with the storefront's market map (D17, D19). It writes nothing, takes 3.1 to 3.5 s, and is `bootstrap`'s step 11 (`= unchanged`, 3.7 to 4.2 s inside bootstrap). Seven things differ from the plan's text or are worth knowing (items 1 to 7 under "What should change in the plan"). The first is a decision: **Redis is live in a bootstrapped setup, so "every integration on fallback" holds for the six others, and for all seven with `REDIS_URL` blank.** The fourth is a flake in Payload's CLI that this task found and that also touches `pnpm bootstrap`.

### What `pnpm run doctor` does

`node scripts/doctor.ts [--online] [--json] [--strict]`, from the repo root. Exit 0 when there is no error (with `--strict`: no warning either), 1 on an error, 2 on a bad option. `--json` prints only JSON (`ok`, `strict`, `online`, `summary`, the seven integrations, every check with `id`, `level`, `message`, `details`, `fix`); a value from an `.env` file is never in it, only key names (a test feeds a sentinel secret through, and a run compared the JSON with twelve secret-looking values from the real `.env` files: no hit).

**Where it reads the environment:** each app's `.env` file, with the shell's variables over it (dotenv's rule, as bootstrap's preflight does). `.env.local` and the other `.env.*` files are not read. Nothing needs a file to exist: on Vercel or Railway the values come from the process.

**Integrations** (`getIntegrationStatus(env)`, pure, `scripts/lib/integrations.ts`; it moves into a package in M4 for the Payload panel): per SPEC §10's "turns on when" keys, grouped by app, with the fallback named.

| | `configured` | `wired` (the code exists) | `state` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stripe, Resend, PostHog, Sentry, Meilisearch, Cloudflare R2 | every key of every part is set | **no, arrives in M6** | always `fallback` in M0 (the table says "the keys are set, but the … code arrives in M6") |
| Redis | `REDIS_URL` is set | yes (T06) | `live` when set |

`half` is true when some keys are set and not all (a warning names the missing ones per app). Sentry's `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is optional (it only uploads source maps). R2 and Resend need their keys in both apps.

| Check | Level | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `postgres` (one per server) | error | reachable by the protocol, and the port is this project's container (D15), as bootstrap's preflight (the code is shared: `inspectTargets` in `checks.ts`) |
| `redis` | error | only when `REDIS_URL` is set. The message says the backend never starts while it is down (T06 item 7) |
| `meilisearch`, `mailpit-smtp`, `mailpit-ui` | warning | nothing uses them before M3 and M6; an error once the Meilisearch provider is wired |
| `docker` | warning | one sentence when Docker can't be asked, naming every port that could not be checked |
| `medusa`, `storefront` | warning if not running on this machine; error if they answer ≥ 400 or a remote host does not answer | `GET {MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL}/health`, `GET {NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL}/` (30 s allowed: the first request to `next dev` compiles the page). Warning, because bootstrap runs doctor before anything is started |
| `secrets` | warning (error with `NODE_ENV=production`) | `JWT_SECRET`, `COOKIE_SECRET`, `PAYLOAD_SECRET` blank |
| `publishable-key` | warning missing; error if it does not start `pk_` | the `apk_…` id is not the token |
| `payload-key` | warning | `PAYLOAD_API_KEY` or `PAYLOAD_SERVER_URL` blank in the backend |
| `email-verification` | warning | sign-up requires verification (SPEC §10.2) and Resend is not on |
| `vercel-storage` | warning | `VERCEL`, `VERCEL_ENV` or `VERCEL_URL` is set and the storefront has no `S3_*` keys; absent elsewhere |
| `redis-production` | warning | `NODE_ENV=production` and `REDIS_URL` blank: in-memory event bus, events never leave the process (T06 item 7) |
| `integration-<id>` | warning | half configured |
| `markets-global` | warning empty or unusable; error if Payload can't read it | read with `payload run src/scripts/doctor-payload.ts`: `findGlobal` plus `marketMapProblems`, the same test `getMarketMap()` uses |
| `markets-sync` | warning | D17/D19, offline: `config/markets.ts` against the map (the global when it has countries, else `@shopblok/fixtures`): an open country with no region, a region country the map doesn't serve, a language whose `medusaLocale` isn't in `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`, and the file against itself (a region in a currency the store lacks, a country in two regions) |
| `payload-auth` (`--online`) | error if refused, warning if the storefront is down | `GET {PAYLOAD_SERVER_URL}/api/integrations/me` with the key: the user must be in `integrations` |
| `medusa-markets` (`--online`) | warning; error if Medusa refuses the key | `GET /store/regions` and `/store/locales` with the storefront's key: every open country has a region, every language's locale is in the store |
| `medusa-orphans` (`--online`) | warning | a region whose countries `config/markets.ts` no longer lists (item 6) |
| `search-index` (`--online`) | warning | `POST /store/search` with a `stats` facet on `min_price_<currency>` for each region's currency; a currency without the field says `run medusa db:migrate and restart the backend` |

| The tests can fail | **Pass** | 46 mutations, each applied to the committed file and reverted, each failing a test: `isSet` without `trim`, `every` → `some`, `live` without `wired`, `half` for any key, Redis not wired, Sentry's token required, "each app" for one app, a key read from the other app; the email warning inverted, the Vercel and production checks forced or inverted, the `pk_` prefix not checked, production secrets only a warning, each of the two "missing from the index" messages, any `200` as present, another currency's message counted, `user: null` as ok, any user as the integration, locales and regions not compared, the "database does not exist" cause; each D17 rule (no region, region not served, locale, closed countries, unused languages, a country in two regions); `--strict` a no-op, an error not failing, a message or a detail not redacted; a redirect followed, a refusal not recognised (plain and inside an aggregate), no timeout; silence counting failures, no retry, no attempts limit; a URL password with an empty user; Docker asked about a remote host or once per target; Node's banner taken as the reason; an orphan found by one country, a region with no countries counted, matching by name. **The first pass caught 41 of 44** (before the orphan check existed): the three misses were the refusal inside an aggregate error and Node's banner (two tests added: `codeOf`, a thrown string) and a `--disable-warning` flag on the child that did nothing, since its stderr is captured anyway (removed). The three orphan mutations were added with that check |

### Changes from the review of this PR

A code review of the diff made 15 points. Each is fixed here, with a test that fails on the old code (the new tests are 83 of the 270), except where it says otherwise.

- **`localhost` with an unusable IPv6 (a default Docker or CI container).** Node reports a refused connection on both addresses as an `AggregateError` whose own `code` is the first address's, here `EAFNOSUPPORT`; `codeOf` read that and called a stopped server `unreachable`, which made a stopped Medusa an error and could fail bootstrap's step 11 on a fresh setup. A refusal on any address now wins.
- **`--online` dropped a whole check when `/store/locales` failed.** It now reports a warning with the reason (HTTP status, timeout or refusal) and still compares the regions with the storefront's map. The three `--online` requests that do not depend on each other (Payload, Medusa regions, Medusa locales) run at once.
- **Staging was treated as development.** The backend takes a blank `JWT_SECRET` or `COOKIE_SECRET` only for `NODE_ENV` unset, `development` or `test` (`DEFAULT_SECRET_ENVS`) and refuses to start for anything else; doctor now makes every other value an error, not only `production`.
- **The search-index check passed for no currencies** ("exists for ."). It now warns that nothing was checked. A currency that a leftover region shares with a region in the file is no longer left out of the check.
- **A Redis that needs a password, or `rediss://`, failed the protocol probe.** `-NOAUTH` counts as a Redis (`-LOADING` still does not), and a `rediss://` URL is probed over TLS. This also fixes bootstrap's preflight, which uses the same probe. The TLS path is covered only by a negative test (a plain server fails the handshake); there is no certificate in the repo to serve a positive one.
- **A wrong `PAYLOAD_SERVER_URL` was blamed on the key.** A 200 that is not Payload's JSON (a proxy's page, another app) is `unknown`, not `rejected`, and its fix text points at the address; only `"user": null` means the key is not known.
- **`pnpm` that could not start** was reported as "Payload failed without a message". The spawn error, a signal and the exit code are named now (`explainPayloadRun`).
- **`redact` left part of a password with an `@` or `/` in it.** The password now runs to the last `@` of the word, up to a `?` or `#`.
- **The decisions in `doctor.ts` had no tests.** `isRequired`, `checkIdOf`, `serviceChecks`, `serverCheck` and the skip-Payload rule moved to `scripts/lib/doctor-services.ts`; the `--online`, markets-global and markets-sync checks are built by pure functions in `doctor-checks.ts` and `markets-check.ts`, so `doctor.ts` only looks at the machine. Each check's id and title are written once.
- **Payload's silent-exit workaround is in one place.** `runPayload` in `exec.ts` starts every Payload CLI command; bootstrap and doctor both use it.
- **Doctor waited for things in a row.** The `markets` global is read as soon as the Postgres check is done, not after the storefront answers (up to 30 s on a cold `next dev`); the `--online` requests go out with the first wave instead of after it; `docker info` is asked once for all services, not once per service (`inspectTargets` does it, as the `observeDocker` probe).
- **`wired` and the blank-secret rule could drift from the backend.** `backend-gates.test.ts` loads the backend's `buildModules` and `getSecret` in place and fails if an integration whose backend keys change the registered modules is not marked wired (the day M6 adds a gate and the flag stays `false`; checked by setting Redis to `false`), or if doctor and `getSecret` disagree about which `NODE_ENV` refuses a blank secret (checked by narrowing doctor's list). It is its own file so that a failure to load Medusa's framework fails only these tests.
- **A `Target` knows which databases it holds** (`databases`), so doctor no longer finds the payload database by looking for "payload" in a label.
- **Shared helpers instead of copies:** `hostOf` (checks.ts), `envValue` (env-file.ts, the shell-over-`.env` rule bootstrap and doctor share), `isSet` (integrations.ts), and Meilisearch's probe uses `request()`.
- **Bootstrap's step 11 and a server that is already running: not changed.** The review feared that a dev server left running across a `docker compose down -v` answers 5xx after bootstrap rewrote the publishable key, and that doctor's error then fails a run whose real steps all passed. That needs a server started before the wipe and kept through it; CLOUD.md's flow (a new container, a paused session) has none. The fix would be a doctor option that bootstrap passes, for a case nobody has met. What was cheap and is done: bootstrap prints "The publishable key changed: rebuild the storefront" when any later step fails too, not only at the end. If the case turns up, the option is the way.

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 5 and 7 are applied in this PR (code, plan text); 6 is fixed in doctor; 8 to 11 are notes for the tasks named.

1. **Redis is live after bootstrap, so "every integration on fallback" is not true of the default setup.** T14's Verify and T16 step 2 (`pnpm run doctor --json` shows every integration on fallback) and the exit criterion §7 item 5 assumed a keyless setup puts all seven on fallback. SPEC §10 says Redis turns on with `REDIS_URL` ("set locally by default"), T06 wired it, and bootstrap writes it, so it is `live` in any bootstrapped setup, and that is true: the backend really runs on Redis. Meilisearch is `fallback` for another reason (item 2). **Decision (mine, easy to change in `integrations.ts`):** the claim becomes "every *third-party* integration (Stripe, Resend, PostHog, Sentry, R2) is on its fallback, and so is Meilisearch until M6", which is `integrations.filter(i => i.kind === "third-party")` in `--json`; with `REDIS_URL` blank all seven are. T16's assertion and §7 item 5 are changed in the plan.
2. **Keys for a provider that is not built yet don't make it live.** `.env.example` sets `MEILISEARCH_HOST` and `MEILISEARCH_API_KEY`, which are SPEC §10's "turns on when" keys, but the backend still searches with `search-postgres` until M6 (the preflight and `/store/search` agree), and the same holds for Stripe, Resend, PostHog, Sentry and R2 if someone sets their keys now. Reporting `live` would be wrong, so each integration has a `wired` flag in `scripts/lib/integrations.ts` (Redis `true`, the rest `false`, `arrives: "M6"`). M6 flips it. The output says "the keys are set, but the Meilisearch code arrives in M6".
3. **A server that is not running on this machine is a warning, not an error.** The plan's default probes include Medusa `/health` and the storefront `/`, and bootstrap's step 11 runs doctor right after setup, when nothing is started: an error would fail every bootstrap (the hand-over said so). A refused connection on `localhost` is therefore a warning with `Start it with `pnpm dev``; an answer ≥ 400, or a remote host that doesn't answer, is an error. `--strict` makes the warning fail, which is the go-live check. Likewise Meilisearch and Mailpit are warnings when down (nothing uses them yet); Postgres and Redis are errors.
4. **Payload's CLI exits 0 without running anything in about 1 run in 20, and bootstrap is affected.** `payload run` and `payload migrate` start through `tsx`'s `module.register` hooks; in 2 to 3 of every 60 runs the process leaves by itself, exit code 0, 2.5 s after it started, with no output at all, not even the `No email adapter provided` line every real run prints (measured above, with and without pnpm, with and without `NODE_ENV=production`, to a pipe and to a file; `beforeExit` fires and nothing raises). The cause is in the loader or in Node 22.22.0 and was not found. **What it did in T13's code:** step 8 reports `It printed no {"bootstrapPayload":…} result line` and fails (loud, a re-run fixes it), but **step 7 (`payload migrate`) would read the empty output as `= unchanged` and skip a migration silently** (by reading the code; not seen happen); on a fresh database step 8 then fails, on an existing one with a pending migration nothing says so. **Fix (applied):** `exitedInSilence(result)` and `retryWhileSilent(attempt, 3)` in `scripts/lib/exec.ts`: a command that exits 0, isn't stopped and printed nothing is run again, up to three times. Bootstrap's `payload()` helper and doctor's `payload run` use it; a real failure, a prompt, a timeout or any output is returned at once. T16's `e2e-smoke` runs `pnpm bootstrap --ci` and will meet the flake about once in 20 runs, which is now absorbed.
5. **The search index answers a missing currency in two ways, and the plan only knew one.** `Unknown field "min_price_brl"` is what Medusa says for a currency the store doesn't have; the case D17 is about (a region and currency seeded, no `db:migrate`) answers `Field "min_price_brl" is not facetable on the postgres search provider`. Found only by running the scenario (the plan's own recipe: add `brl` to a copy of `config/markets.ts`, don't migrate). `classifySearchField` treats both as missing, a `200` with no facet as missing, and anything else (another status or message, for example after a Medusa upgrade) as "could not tell", never as a currency problem. Each region currency gets its own request, so the message names the currency.
6. **A market taken out of `config/markets.ts` leaves its region in Medusa.** T13's "the edit undone" check looked at `store_currency` and `store_locale`; the `Brazil` region and its tax region stay, because `seed-markets.ts` adds and never removes. Doctor now reports it (`medusa-orphans`, found by countries so an admin's rename doesn't matter) and leaves the currency out of the search-index check. Deleting the region in the admin works (`DELETE /admin/regions/{id}` → `deleted: true`). T13's text "`brl` and `pt-BR` removed" is about the store only.
7. **The `markets` global is read with `payload run`, as a production process.** SQL through `docker compose exec` would only work for the compose database and would copy table and column names; `payload run src/scripts/doctor-payload.ts` reads the global the way `getMarketMap()` does (`findGlobal`, `mapFromGlobal`, `marketMapProblems`), works against any `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` and costs 2.5 s. Its risk is Payload's creation of a missing database on connect (dev only, D18), so doctor runs it with `NODE_ENV=production` (`disableCreateDatabase` follows it), and not at all when the Postgres check failed (a foreign server). Both are tested live above. The D17 map is that global when it has countries; the fixtures only when it is empty or unreadable (the dev fallback `getMarketMap()` uses). The backend's file and the fixtures are read by a child `node` process (`loadExports`): `tsc` refuses the backend's file (T13 item 6), `@shopblok/fixtures`' `index.ts` has an extensionless relative import that Node can't resolve, and a child keeps Node's reparse warning for the typeless package off doctor's output. The shapes are written in `markets-check.ts` and checked at run time.
8. **For T15:** `doctor` in `package.json` is `node scripts/doctor.ts`, which is right; nothing else changes. `pnpm run doctor` prints pnpm's `ELIFECYCLE` line when it exits 1.
9. **For T16:** the `e2e-smoke` assertion is `pnpm run doctor --json` with `integrations.filter(i => i.kind === "third-party")` all `fallback` (item 1), and `ok` true; the job starts the servers before it, so Medusa and the storefront probes are green and `--online` can be added: `payload-auth`, `medusa-markets` and `search-index` then run for real. `check` doesn't run doctor (no stack).
10. **For T17 (SPEC v0.6):** (a) §10's table lists `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` as a turn-on key; it only uploads source maps, so doctor treats it as optional. (b) §10 says the R2 public URL is `S3_PUBLIC_URL`, §15's backend row says `S3_FILE_URL`; doctor reads `S3_PUBLIC_URL` in both apps; M6 should pick one. (c) §7.6's status panel gets `getIntegrationStatus` with its `wired` flag; M6 flips the flags. (d) §10.2/§16: `pnpm run doctor`, and `--strict` is the "all green" check. (e) "Redis: in-memory fallback" is live in dev and a production warning (`redis-production`).
11. **For W2:** `pnpm run doctor` and `pnpm run doctor --online` on Windows (the plan already lists them); expect `payload run` to take longer, and a multi-line `--eval` argument in `loadExports` is the one thing never run there.

### Smaller observations

- **Time:** doctor 3.1 to 3.5 s alone; `payload run` is 2.5 s of it, and the rest runs in parallel (sockets, five `docker compose port` calls, two HTTP calls, two child reads). Inside bootstrap it adds 3.7 to 4.2 s (a second run: 37.9 s in all). Its table is about 40 lines in bootstrap's output; there is no `--brief`.
- **The first request to `next dev` is slow** (it compiles `/`), hence the 30 s allowed for the storefront; against `next start` it is 20 ms.
- **`localhost` can resolve to two addresses**, and a refusal on both arrives as an `AggregateError` whose error is on `cause.errors`; `request()` reads both shapes (the Node 22.22.0 run needed only the first, so the second is tested with a synthetic error).
- **Doctor doesn't check that the databases exist or are migrated** for Medusa (a running `/health` shows it connected), and for Payload only through the global read. A table-level check would need a Postgres client, which `scripts/` doesn't have.
- **`--online` sends seven or eight requests** to the running servers (`/api/integrations/me`, `/store/regions`, `/store/locales`, one `/store/search` per region currency: four or five). None writes.
- **Bootstrap after the Brazil edit was undone** printed `[3/11] … ✓ done (search index rebuilt on the next start)` and `[4/11] … ✓ done (markets seeded, demo catalog unchanged)`, as T13 measured; the `Brazil` region stayed (item 6).
- **Throwaways** (the mutation harness `mutate.py`, the flake loops, the `docker` shim, the Brazil edit and its original) were in the session's scratch directory; the numbers above are from them.

## Root scripts and the dev loop (T15)

**Result: every check in T15's Verify list passes. T16 can start.** All eleven root scripts run and do what T15's table says, so no script changed. The one change is a line in `turbo.json`: `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY` is now in `globalPassThroughEnv`, because Turborepo's strict environment mode dropped it and `pnpm dev` then queued Medusa telemetry even with the opt-out set in the shell (found by reading what reaches the servers, then A/B-tested in three runs). The other findings are about the dev loop itself: what `--filter=@shopblok/storefront...` starts, what a shell variable can and cannot do under `pnpm dev`, and how to stop it (Ctrl-C in a terminal; `kill -INT` of the turbo process in a session without one).

CLOUD.md's step 3: `dockerd` started, `pnpm infra:up` 15.9 s with 4 healthy, `docker compose port postgres 5432` printed `0.0.0.0:15432`. Step 4: `pnpm bootstrap` 49.3 s, 8 done, 2 unchanged, 2 skipped (doctor's three warnings: Medusa and the storefront not running yet, no Resend key) |

Medusa reads the variable in `@medusajs/telemetry`'s `Store` (`this.disabled_ = isTruthy(process.env.MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY)`). In `@medusajs/medusa` 2.21.2, `medusa start` tracks `CLI_START`, `CLI_START_COMPLETED` and `PING` (`medusa develop` only builds a `Store` and spawns it) and `medusa user` tracks `CLI_USER` and `CLI_USER_COMPLETED`; no other file in `dist/commands/` (36 files, `build` and `db/` among them) imports `@medusajs/telemetry`. What those commands call in other packages was not searched. **After the review of this PR moved the entry from the `dev` task to `globalPassThroughEnv`, A, B and C were repeated (A on the old file, B and C on the new one) with the same results** (stack up, `~/.config/medusa` removed before each; `/health` 200 after 8 to 9 s; stopped with `kill -INT` of the turbo PID, 0 processes left after 2 s). Also: every task's hash (`turbo run build --dry=json`) is the same with the variable `true`, `false` and unset, and a probe task in a scratch workspace saw the variable with `globalPassThroughEnv` and with a task-level `passThroughEnv`, and not without either |
| A variable that is in the shell and in `.env` | **Measured** | `DATABASE_URL=postgres://postgres:postgres@localhost:1/bogus pnpm dev --filter=@shopblok/backend`: `/health` 200 after 9 s, and the backend's environment held the `.env` value (`localhost:15432/medusa`). A variable turbo drops, like this one, never reaches the server, so `.env` is the only source. What turbo does pass through beats `.env`, because `@next/env` does not override a variable that is already set (checked: a shell `NEXT_PUBLIC_T15` and a plain variable both read `from-shell`, the `.env` value lost). That is `PAYLOAD_*`, `MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL`, `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` in the storefront (Turborepo infers it for Next.js) and the built-in set (`HOME`, `PATH`, …). So a stale `NEXT_PUBLIC_MEDUSA_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` left exported in a shell beats the key in `.env` under `pnpm dev`, and bootstrap rotates that key after a volume wipe. `pnpm bootstrap` and `pnpm run doctor` read it the other way (shell over `.env`) for every variable |
| Stopping `pnpm dev`, in a terminal | **Pass** | A Python `pty.fork()` run of the full `pnpm dev` (ready in 14 to 15 s): 12 processes in its session; after a `^C` byte, 0 left after 1 s. (Once the pages have compiled Next adds five or six Turbopack `pool_entry` workers; T12 counted 13 PIDs at another moment.) **One run, on a freshly started stack, before any page had compiled; the six `kill -INT` runs below are the repeated ones.** Not measured: the first `^C` on a PC that has Claude Code installed. `medusa develop` answers SIGINT with `promptClaudeCodePlugin()` (`@medusajs/medusa` 2.21.2, `utils/claude-code-plugin.js`), which asks a one-time question ("install the Medusa Plugin for Claude Code?", default yes) when stdout is a terminal, `CI` is not `true`, `~/.claude` exists (or `CLAUDE_CODE` is set) and the plugin is not installed; it records that it asked before it asks. The pty run was not held up by it, but why (a flag stored in `~/.config/medusa`, or turbo's TUI) was not checked, so the 1 s holds for this VM only. With no terminal (next row) the handler returns at once |
| Stopping `pnpm dev`, with no terminal (a cloud session) | **Pass** | `kill -INT` of the turbo binary stopped everything in 1 s, in six runs, with nothing left each time. Find it with `ps -eo pid,cmd \| grep '[l]inux-64/bin/turbo run dev'`. A SIGINT sent to the `pnpm dev` process alone did nothing: its signal mask was `SigIgn 0x1007` (HUP, INT, QUIT, PIPE). That is the mask a `setsid nohup … &` launch from a non-interactive shell gives (`nohup` ignores HUP, a background job of a non-interactive shell ignores INT and QUIT, Node ignores PIPE itself), so it is most likely inherited from how this session started `pnpm dev`, not a choice pnpm makes; another launch method was not tried. Turbo puts each task in its own process group, so only a terminal's `^C` or a signal to turbo reaches them |
| Tests | **Unchanged** | No test was added: the change is one JSON key, and what it does is only visible by starting Medusa (the A/B/C runs above); a string match on the JSONC `turbo.json` would not show that Medusa stays quiet. The review of this PR asked for a static guard on the entry and none was added: `scripts/check-workspace.ts` checks the installed dependency graph (React, Payload, workspace links), and the entry has its reason in a comment beside it |
| Prettier on what T15 changed (`--config packages/config/prettier.config.mjs`) | **Pass** | `All matched files use Prettier code style!` for `turbo.json`; `turbo run dev --dry=json` shows `resolvedTaskDefinition.passThroughEnv` of `["MEDUSA_BACKEND_URL","PAYLOAD_*"]` for the dev tasks and `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY` under `globalCacheInputs.environmentVariables.passthrough` |
| `AGENTS.md` | **Pass** | `md5sum` `995f281e6ed512494bf15f386443be6c`, unchanged after every turbo run |
| Stack stopped, CI's state | **Pass** | `pnpm infra:down` run (`docker ps` empty), both `.env` files deleted, then the root checks above ran; `git status` shows only the intended files. No `next`, `medusa`, `turbo` or `pnpm` process left |
| Not run | | Windows (W2): `pnpm dev`, `^C` and the infra scripts there; Node 24; a remote cache; `--filter` by Git range |

### What should change in the plan

Items 1, 2 and 6 are applied in this PR (plan text, `turbo.json`, W2's checklist); the rest are notes.

1. **`infra:down` removes the containers and the network, and keeps the volumes.** T15's table said "stop the services". `docker compose down` does more than stop, and the databases survive it; only `infra:reset` wipes them. The table says so now.
2. **`turbo.json`: `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY` is in `globalPassThroughEnv`.** Without it a developer who has the opt-out in their shell profile is opted back in by `pnpm dev`. (`pnpm bootstrap` does not go through turbo: `exec.ts` starts each command with `{ ...process.env }`, so the shell's variables reach its Medusa commands whole; `medusa user` tracks `CLI_USER` there, and whether it queues them with the variable set was not measured.) It is a passthrough entry, not `env`: its value enters no hash (checked, above), but its name enters the global hash, so adding the entry changed every task's hash once and a warm local `.turbo` cache went cold (checked on `build`, `lint` and `test` with `--root-turbo-json` and the previous file; an empty list gives the old hashes). It is global rather than on `dev` because an opt-out that covers one task silently stops covering a task added later that runs a Medusa command; today only `dev` is known to need it, since `medusa build` and the `db:*` commands import no tracker (above).
3. **Considered and left alone (owner's call, nothing blocks):** (a) `--env-mode=loose` for `dev` only (`turbo run dev --env-mode=loose` in the script), which would make the shell win over `.env` as it does in `bootstrap` and `doctor`; the cache-safety reason for strict mode does not apply to an uncached task, but it also removes the check that a variable is listed, and no real use needs it today. (b) `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` and `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, which are dropped: nothing the dev servers do in M0 goes to the internet except telemetry, so no use exists; M6 (Stripe, Resend, R2) will have one behind a proxy, and the entry belongs there. **Default: as built.**
4. **The scripts package has 270 tests, not 187.** The T14 hand-over (NEXT-SESSION.md) and its prompt still said 187, the count before the review fixes; the T14 section above says 270. Root `pnpm test` is 18 + 270 + 375 + 85.
5. **For T16:** (a) The `check` job is exactly this session's root checks: no `.env`, no stack, `8 / 9 / 4 / 2` tasks, 18 + 270 + 375 + 85 tests. CI starts with a cold cache, so every build there is `cache miss`; the cache hit is only visible in a job that builds twice. (b) The `e2e-smoke` job builds the storefront **after** `pnpm bootstrap --ci` (the key is baked in at build time, T12 item 8); `.env*` is a `build` input, so turbo hashes it again by itself. (c) A server that the job starts directly (`medusa develop`, `next start -p 8000`) gets the whole shell environment and reads its own `.env`: the strict-mode findings above are about `turbo run dev` only. (d) CI has internet access, so `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY: true`, `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: 1` and `TURBO_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: 1` (the three that CLOUD.md sets) belong in the workflow's `env:`. Medusa's `start` (under `medusa develop`) and `user` (in `pnpm bootstrap --ci`) queue events and send them. (e) A runner has no Playwright browser: the workflow runs `pnpm --filter @shopblok/storefront exec playwright install --with-deps chromium`; the cloud's copy is already in `/opt/ms-playwright`, so that line is not run in the VM. (f) With both servers up, `pnpm run doctor --json` gives `ok` true and one warning (Resend).
6. **For W2 (Windows):** `pnpm dev` and its `^C`, the infra scripts and `pnpm build` have only run on Linux; W2's checklist (plan §4a) now lists all three. The passthrough entry does not depend on the platform.

### Smaller observations

- **`/usr/bin/time` is not in the image.** Time a command with the shell's `time` or with `date +%s` before and after.
- **Medusa, Next and Payload each keep a config folder outside the repo after a dev run:** `~/.config/medusa/{config.json, events.json}`, `~/.config/nextjs-nodejs`, `~/.config/payload-nodejs`. Removing `~/.config/medusa` before a run is what makes "did it queue telemetry" a clean question.
- **A short-lived `payload generate:importmap` process** (parent 1) appeared once during `pnpm dev`, started by the Payload plugin inside Next; it was gone by the next listing and by the end of every run.
- **The `generateMetadata()` / `new Date()` / instant-navigation errors** that `next dev` logs for `/admin/[[...segments]]` are the ones T09 listed for S1 (item 7); nothing new.
- **Stream output with no terminal:** a `pnpm dev` started with `setsid nohup … > log` prints every line prefixed with its task and no interface; the interface (`ui: "tui"`) only starts with a terminal. Nothing in `turbo.json` sets `ui`.
- **Commit trailers:** this session's commits say `Co-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>` without a model name, as T12's did, because the instructions forbid model identifiers in pushed artifacts.
- **Throwaways** (`telemetry-ab.sh`, the three-run telemetry test; `pty-ctrlc.py`, the `pty.fork()` run that counts a session's processes and sends `^C`) were in the session's scratch directory; the numbers above are from them.

### The workflow

| Job | Runs on | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `check` | `ubuntu-latest`, Node 22 (`.nvmrc`, required) and Node 24 (`continue-on-error`) | `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `check:workspace`, `typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, `build`; on the Node 22 leg also `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high`, which cannot fail the run. No `.env`, no database: T15's root checks |
| `e2e-smoke` | `ubuntu-latest` | `docker compose up -d --wait`, Playwright's Chromium, `pnpm bootstrap --ci`, `pnpm build`, Medusa (`pnpm --filter @shopblok/backend dev`) and the storefront (`… start`) started directly with `nohup`, `pnpm --filter @shopblok/storefront test:e2e --reporter=list,html` (all three suites), `pnpm --silent run doctor --json` with the assertion below, `pnpm run doctor --online`; uploads the Playwright report and the server logs |
| `windows-gate` → `windows-smoke` | `ubuntu-latest` → `windows-latest` | The gate decides whether the Windows job runs: yes on `schedule` (Mondays 05:43 UTC) and `workflow_dispatch`, and on a PR that changes `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` or any `package.json`; otherwise `windows-smoke` is `skipped`. The Windows job: install, `check:workspace`, `typecheck`, `build` |

Triggers: `push` to `main`, every `pull_request`, `schedule`, `workflow_dispatch`. A new push to a PR cancels that PR's earlier run (`cancel-in-progress` for `pull_request` only; seen once: the revert's run `38034789842` was cancelled by the next push, see the checks below). `permissions: contents: read`; the gate adds `pull-requests: read`. `env:` sets `MEDUSA_DISABLE_TELEMETRY`, `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED` and `TURBO_TELEMETRY_DISABLED`. Every job sets up pnpm and Node with the composite action `.github/actions/setup` (pnpm, `setup-node` with the pnpm store cached, and a line with Node's version, ICU and CLDR: item 2); `check` passes it the matrix's Node version.

**`e2e-smoke`'s assertion** (the `node --eval` step, on `pnpm --silent run doctor --json`): exactly 5 integrations with `kind: "third-party"` (Stripe, Resend, PostHog, Sentry, R2), all `state: "fallback"`, and `ok: true`. It prints `third-party integrations: stripe=fallback resend=fallback posthog=fallback sentry=fallback r2=fallback`. The count is checked too, so a filter that finds nothing cannot pass. Redis is `live` (bootstrap sets `REDIS_URL`). Meilisearch is `kind: "local"`, so it is outside the check, and its `state` is `fallback` until M6, as T14 decided.

**Action pins:** `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-node@v7`, `pnpm/action-setup@v6` (plan §2) and `actions/upload-artifact@v7` (not in §2). A session can reach no repository but this one (`github.com/actions/…` gave 403), so each was looked up on the web before the push: checkout v7.0.0 was released on 2026-06-18, followed by v7.0.1; setup-node has a `v7` tag; `pnpm/action-setup`'s README says "This action supports pnpm v12 and earlier" and that it works with `actions/setup-node`; upload-artifact's latest is v7.0.1. All four resolved on the runner (the first run's "Set up job" and each `Run …` step succeeded), which is the real check.
