### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 4 are applied in this PR (plan text, tests, workflow); 5 to 9 are notes for the tasks named.

1. **`payload-admin` logs in with the credentials bootstrap made; it does not test the first-user form.** The plan said "first user, login, create a localized page". Bootstrap's step 8 has created the first admin, so `/admin/create-first-user` is reachable only on an empty `users` table. Reaching it in CI would take a second, empty database and a second server, and **the second server cannot be the same build on another port**: `payload.config.ts` reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` at build time for `serverURL`, `cors` and `csrf`, and Payload's CSRF check compares that origin with the request's. Measured with the production build on :8001 and the suite pointed at it: login and `GET /api/users/me` work, but a `POST /api/pages` with the same cookie answers **403** (`You are not allowed to perform this action.`), while on :8000 it answers 201. So a second server means a second build too. Against that: the form is Payload's own code, nobody sees it after the quickstart, and what the project adds to it (`firstUserIsAdmin` in `users.ts`) has its unit tests. Logging in with `DEV_ADMIN_EMAIL` and `DEV_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from the backend `.env` (or `E2E_ADMIN_EMAIL` and `E2E_ADMIN_PASSWORD`) tests what the quickstart's reader does, and fails if bootstrap makes an admin that cannot log in. **Not covered by this suite:** the form itself, and `firstUserIsAdmin` through the browser (bootstrap creates its admin with `role: "admin"` through the Local API, so the hook is not what makes it one). **S1's admin suite lists it** (plan §6: "first-user creation, login and logout"), on the spike's own matrix, so S1 is where it gets tested first; M7's deployment check, on a deployment that really starts empty, is the other place. By hand, on a throwaway database (not tried in this task): empty `users` with SQL (Payload's own delete refuses to remove the last admin: `keepOneAdminOnDelete`), open `/admin`, fill the form, and check that `GET /api/users/me` says `role: "admin"`.
2. **A test must not pin what the runtime's CLDR decides.** The first run failed `@shopblok/i18n`'s `formatMoney` tests for `de-CH` (`CHF 1'234.50` where the test expected `CHF 1’234.50`) and `fr-CH` (`1'234.50 CHF` where it expected `1 234.50 CHF`). Node 22.22.0 (the VM, ICU 77.1, CLDR 47) writes `’` (U+2019) and U+202F; Node 22.23.3 (ICU 78.3, CLDR 48), which `.nvmrc`'s `22` resolves to on a runner, writes ASCII `'` for both. The product code is right either way, since `formatMoney` is `Intl.NumberFormat`; the test compares with the no-break spaces turned into plain ones, and now also maps the grouping mark (`’`, U+202F, `'`) between digits to `'`, so a grouping that is lost or written as a space still fails (`plain` in `packages/i18n/src/index.test.ts`, with a comment that says which version writes which). The `wiring` spec avoids the question by taking its expected prices from `Intl.NumberFormat` in the test's own process, which is the server's Node, and by using amounts under 1000 (no grouping). **Rule for later tasks:** an assertion on `Intl` output should compare what the project controls (symbol side, decimal mark, currency) and normalise what CLDR decides. Every job now prints Node, ICU and CLDR (the "Versions" step), and the VM can reproduce a runner's Node by downloading the latest 22.x or 24.x into a scratch folder.
3. **`windows-smoke` sits behind a gate job.** A job cannot have a `paths` filter, and the plan wants Windows minutes spent only weekly and on dependency PRs. `windows-gate` (Linux, 3 to 4 s) decides from the event and, for a PR, from `gh api …/pulls/N/files`. `package.json` is matched at any depth, because a dependency bump changes a workspace's `package.json`. Cost: one more job in every PR's check list, and `windows-smoke` shows as skipped.
4. **Renovate holds back majors that earlier decisions rule out** (`renovate.json`, each rule with its reason): TypeScript (D5), ESLint and `@eslint/js` (T02 item 1), `@types/node` (the 22 line), `graphql` (Payload 3 allows 16), Node (`.nvmrc` and `engines`), and any major of a compose image (a database major is a data migration). `config:recommended` does not pin Actions to digests (checked in Renovate's preset list), so the tag pins stay. Without these rules the first Renovate run would propose TypeScript 7, ESLint 10 and `@types/node` 26. The groups are `@medusajs/*`, `payload` with `@payloadcms/*`, and `next` with `eslint-config-next`; `e2e-smoke`, with the admin suite, runs on every PR, so it runs on the Payload and Next ones.
5. **For you, G3 (nothing here can change them).** (a) **Secret scanning and push protection:** `GET /repos/…` answers `security_and_analysis: null` and `…/secret-scanning/alerts` is blocked by the proxy, so the session cannot see whether either is on. Turn them on in Settings → Code security; a private repo may need GitHub Secret Protection (paid), and if the page says so, that is the answer to record. (b) **Dependabot alerts are disabled** on the repo (`…/dependabot/alerts` answers "Dependabot alerts are disabled for this repository"); Renovate's PRs and the non-blocking `pnpm audit` step are what is left, unless you turn them on. (c) **Install the Renovate GitHub app** on the repo; `renovate.json` does nothing without it, and its first run will open a dashboard issue and the pin and group PRs. (d) **Branch protection:** `main` is not protected (C0). If you want CI to gate merges, require `check (Node 22)` and `e2e-smoke`, not `check (Node 24)` (informational) or `windows-smoke` (often skipped; a skipped required check counts as passing). (e) `windows-smoke` did not need the Actions tab: the dispatch worked from the session.
6. **Reading CI from the cloud.** `gh api …/actions/jobs/<id>/logs` redirects to `productionresultssa11.blob.core.windows.net`, which the proxy refuses (`Forbidden`). The GitHub MCP server's `get_job_logs` (`owner`, `repo`, `job_id`, `return_content: true`, `tail_lines`) returned the failed step's log; it has no way to read from the top, so a `Versions`-style line at the start of a long log is out of reach. Run and job states, steps with timings, artifacts' names and sizes, and `…/dispatches` all work through `gh api`. CLOUD.md says so now.
7. **Findings about Payload's admin that the suite works around** (S1 may want them): (a) Next 16 with Cache Components keeps the route you came from mounted but hidden, so right after a redirect `input[name="title"]` matches the old form as well (a strict-mode violation); the spec's `field()` helper selects `:visible`. (b) Payload stores the language an admin last picked, per user (`payload_preferences`, key `locale`), and opens the next page in it: a draft made "in English" landed in `el` after an earlier Greek session. The spec names `?locale=en` in the URL (which also updates the preference) and puts it back at the end. A first creation of that preference can race: a duplicated `locale` row appeared once during exploration (two parallel requests), which is a Payload matter and not seen in the suites. (c) The admin asks `www.gravatar.com` for an avatar (D18); the cloud proxy refuses it (`connect_rejected`), the page is unaffected.
8. **`e2e-smoke` leaves `pnpm build` as the root script,** which also builds the backend (about 30 s of the 97 s on the runner) although only the storefront needs the post-bootstrap build. `pnpm --filter @shopblok/storefront build` would save it; left as the plan words it, so the workflow runs what the quickstart runs.
9. **For T17:** the quickstart can quote CI as the clean-machine reference: on `ubuntu-latest`, after `pnpm install` (18 to 26 s), `docker compose up -d --wait` took 16 s, `pnpm bootstrap --ci` 57 to 60 s, and the production build 94 to 99 s. SPEC §14's "CI uses compose instead of `services:`" is as built (§8 item 13). The Windows install on a runner took 59 s and the build 89 s, with `LongPathsEnabled` as the runner image has it (not checked) and a short workspace path (`D:\a\shopblok-starter\shopblok-starter`), so it does **not** replace W2's long-path check.

### Smaller observations

- **Not exercised:** the `schedule` trigger (it runs only on the default branch, so after the merge), a PR that changes a dependency file (the gate's `true` path ran only against a stub, above), the `push` to `main` trigger, Renovate itself, and a run with a Playwright failure (what the uploaded `test-results` traces look like).
- **`continue-on-error` jobs:** the informational Node 24 job would show as a red check if it failed, without failing the run (not exercised). The audit step used `continue-on-error` too, and was recorded as `conclusion: success` on the runner (1 s) whatever it found; the review of this PR changed it to `pnpm audit … || echo "::warning …"`, so a failed audit is a warning on the run's page.
- **A script that contains `rm -rf` is refused** by a safety check that cannot read it (a rehearsal script run through `bash -c` was; nothing in it ran). The rehearsal scripts then used `turbo --force` for a cold cache, and the build folders were deleted by plain `rm -rf` commands of explicit paths. (A foreground `sleep` of 240 s ran fine here, unlike T12's note.)
- **Cold-cache runner timings are the ones to quote:** `check (Node 22)` 2 m 19 s to 3 m 08 s over three runs; `e2e-smoke` 4 m 17 s to 4 m 54 s; `windows-smoke` 4 m 16 s.
- **`turbo run` on the runner printed `cache miss, executing` for every task**, as the plan expected; the second build in a job is the only place a hit is visible.
- **A `git revert -q` that created nothing** followed by `git commit --amend` rewrote the temporary breaking commit locally instead of reverting it; the push was refused as a non-fast-forward, nothing reached the remote, and the branch was reset to the remote head and reverted properly. The history on the PR is the honest one.
- **Throwaways** (the mutation harness `mutate.py` and its list, the job step-runner `run-job.py`, the exploration scripts, the downloaded Node folders) were in the session's scratch directory; the numbers above are from them.

### Changes from the review of this PR (T16)

A code review of the diff made 15 points; the fixes are in the diff (the audit warns instead of hiding behind `continue-on-error`, `payload-admin`'s cleanup by slug, credentials and status check, the Swiss `formatMoney` expectations, the pnpm major hold-back, the `.github/actions/setup` composite action, the server wait that stops when a server exits, compose diagnostics, two contradictions in this file). Checked against the running stack (`docker compose up`, `pnpm bootstrap --ci`, `pnpm build`, the workflow's own server step, Playwright: 41 passed), with the redirect wait made to fail after a successful save (the draft, page 2, was gone afterwards: `select count(*) from pages` gave 0), and the i18n tests under Node 22.23.3 (CLDR 48).

- **Worth knowing.** A page made in `beforeAll` is traced like any other (the review thought not): `trace.zip` was written for a failing test. It holds the login request, so the admin password is in it; in CI that password belongs to a database that is gone with the runner, so nothing is added.
- **Not a fault.** The plan says Meilisearch "is `fallback` until M6" and the workflow said "`local`": both are right, `local` is its `kind` and `fallback` its `state`; the wording now says which.
- **Left as it is, on purpose.** The root `pnpm build` in `e2e-smoke` (item 8). The `windows-gate` job (item 3): adding `scripts/`, `turbo.json`, `.nvmrc` and `ci.yml` to what it matches is a choice about Windows minutes, which cost double on a private repo, and the weekly run catches the rest; a separate workflow with a `paths` filter is a fair alternative that was not taken. The `thirdParty.length !== 5` assertion changes only when SPEC §10's list does, and `scripts/lib/integrations.test.ts` tests the list itself. The remaining CLDR-pinned expectations (`₮`, `R$`, `Οκτ`, `oct.`) held on CLDR 47 and 48; the next CLDR change that moves one is the signal to normalise it. The temporary break commit `1406356` and its revert stay in the history; PRs merge with a merge commit, so `git bisect --first-parent` skips them.

## Cache Components × Payload admin (S1)

**Result: S1 ran and its evidence is complete; the verdict needs one decision from the owner.** In production mode (`next build && next start`) the base config passes all 19 checks, and so does every other column, with zero console errors, so no edit to Payload's generated files and no change to `next.config.ts` is needed. In `next dev` every column with Cache Components on logs console errors (Next's dev-only prerender validation, and a React "Maximum update depth exceeded" loop in Payload's document and logout views), while the same admin with `cacheComponents` off is clean. The plan's PASS rule says "zero browser-console errors … in both dev and start", so read to the letter S1 is a FAIL and the fallback (`cacheComponents: false`) applies. The ADR proposes to **keep** Cache Components and says why; it lists the fallback's steps if the owner holds the strict reading. [ADR 0001](adr/0001-cache-components-payload.md) has the tables.

| | |
|---|---|
| Date | 2026-10-10 |
| Branch | `claude/busy-fermat-3wb1z5`. The plan names `spike/s1-…` (D13); the session's branch was fixed by the platform, so the prototype is one commit, **`dbfec6e`**, and the next commit removes it. Cut `spike/s1-cache-components` from `dbfec6e` if you want the plan's branch |
| Session start | `node -v` v22.22.0, `pnpm -v` 12.10.1, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` 21.3 s. CLOUD.md's step 3: `dockerd` started, `pnpm infra:up` 17.7 s with 4 healthy, `docker compose port postgres 5432` printed `0.0.0.0:15432`. Step 4: `pnpm bootstrap` 51.1 s (8 done, 2 unchanged, 2 skipped; doctor's three warnings). `playwright --version` 1.64.0, `eslint --version` v9.39.5. Turborepo docs (2.11.7, `docs/README.md`) read; no turbo config changed |
| Files in the merged tree | `docs/adr/0001-cache-components-payload.md`, this section, the plan, NEXT-SESSION. **No code differs from `main`** (`git diff 1aed983 -- . ':!docs'` is empty) |
| Prototype (`dbfec6e`, not for main) | `src/lib/spike/s1.ts` (two cached Local API reads on `cached()`, a loader counter in `globalThis`), `[market]/spike-cache/{page,draft/page}.tsx`, `next/spike/{counter,revalidate,draft}/route.ts`, `payload/hooks/revalidate-pages.ts` (the `pages` `afterChange` hook), a `heroImage` upload field on `pages` with its migration (for the drawer check), and `spike/s1/`: the 19-check Playwright suite, `run-column.sh` (one matrix column, any of `start`/`dev`, `partialPrefetching`, W1/W2/W3, `manual-warning`, Cache Components off), the three `.patch` files, `fresh-db.sh`, the mutation runners. `next.config.ts` carries the `S1_*` toggles there |
| Runs | About 50 suite runs: the plan's 8 columns three times as the suite was corrected, then W2, W3, `manual-warning`, repeats of the base dev column, 3 runs of the Cache Components-off baseline, 4 mutation runs. Logs were kept in the session's scratchpad, not in the repo; the ADR has the numbers of the last run of each column |
| Reproduce | `git switch --detach dbfec6e`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm infra:up`, then from `apps/storefront`: `S1_OUT=<folder> spike/s1/run-column.sh start on no` (arguments: `start`/`dev`, `on`/`off`, `no`/`yes` for W1; `S1_W2=yes`, `S1_W3=yes`, `S1_INSIGHTS=manual`, `S1_CC=off` select the others). It creates `payload_s1` itself and never touches the `payload` database; it refuses to run if `PAYLOAD_DATABASE_URL` is not `…/payload_s1` |

### Checks (S1's PASS list)

| # | PASS criterion | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `next build` exits 0; no blocking-route, uncached-outside-Suspense or sync-I/O error | **Pass** in all 7 `start` columns (35–40 s). Also: a read with `cacheLife` all zero **fails** the build, which is the guard working |
| 2 | Admin suite, zero console errors or hydration warnings | `start`: **pass** in all 7 columns. `dev`: functional checks pass (18 of 19) in the base column, the console check **fails in every column** |
| 3 | `GET /api/pages` 200 | **Pass**. `/admin/nope` answers 200 (accepted) |
| 4 | Cache behaviour: hit counter, fresh within 2 requests, `{ expire: 0 }` | **Pass** in `start` (the "2 requests" needs a 500 ms pause with `'max'`, below) |
| 5 | Draft isolation, 200 concurrent mixed requests | **Pass**: 0 of 100 public responses carried the draft marker, cold and warm; every draft response had it |
| 6 | Market home `◐` with a streamed dynamic hole | **Pass**: `/[market]` and `/admin/[[...segments]]` are `◐`; `/` has the pending boundary and fallback in its shell |

### Checks beyond S1's list

- **The first-user form is now tested** (T16 item 1 left it open): the suite runs against an empty `payload_s1` and its own server on :8000, so the origin Payload's CSRF check compares is the right one. It passes in `start` and `dev`. It is not in CI: CI's database already has the bootstrap admin.
- **A baseline with Cache Components off** (dev, 3 runs): 10 of 10 admin checks, 0 console messages. This is what turns "Payload logs errors in dev" into "Cache Components × Payload logs errors in dev". It needed `globalNotFound` off and the storefront's `use cache` pages set aside, since they cannot compile without the flag.
- **The tests can fail:** four mutations, each caught by the intended check (ADR 0001).
- **Fresh content with `'max'`:** request 1 stale, the refresh takes about 300 ms, request 2 after 500 ms is fresh. Back to back, request 2 is still stale.
- **`withPayload` and `turbopackServerFastRefresh`** (plan §6, T00): `withPayload` sets `experimental.turbopackServerFastRefresh: false` ("Server fast refresh breaks HMR" in its source), which is why the banner prints `⨯ turbopackServerFastRefresh`. Dev only; nothing for a build.
- **`PAYLOAD_CACHE_COMPONENTS_ENABLED`:** `withPayload` sets it when `nextConfig.cacheComponents` is truthy, and `RootLayout` wraps its content in `<Suspense fallback={null}>` only then. Our config goes through `withPayload(buildConfig(phase))`, so it is set.

### What should change in the plan

Items 1 to 3 are applied in this PR (plan §4, §6, §4a); the rest are notes.

1. **S1's PASS rule needs a clause about dev.** As written it cannot pass with Cache Components on: dev logs errors that `start` does not. The owner decides whether "zero console errors" means production mode only (ADR 0001 proposes yes) or both. Until then the plan says S1 is *run, undecided*.
2. **`partialPrefetching` stays on, and none of W1, W2, W3 or `manual-warning` is adopted.** `check-workspace.ts` therefore asserts nothing new (the plan asked for an assertion only if a generated-file edit is adopted), and the plan's "Renovate PRs for `payload` or `next` run the admin suite" is already true through T16's `payload-admin`.
3. **"Fresh content appears within 2 requests"** (criterion 4) means: request 1 may be stale and starts a refresh of about 300 ms; request 2 follows after a pause.
4. **For M1 and M4 (`cached()`):** tags are fixed when `cached()` is called. SPEC §8.3 asks for global tags, which works (`payload:pages`). A tag per language needs one wrapper per language or `cached()` taking `tags` as a function of its arguments; decide when a page needs it.
5. **For M4 (the `pages` hook):** `revalidateTag(tag, 'max')` works inside Payload's REST route handler (the admin's save), `updateTag` does not. It throws outside Next (scripts, `payload migrate`), so wrap it. Do not call it for drafts: revalidate when the document or its previous version is published.
6. **For T17:** SPEC §8.3, Q14 and the risk-table row change as listed under "Spec changes" in ADR 0001. The ADR's template (context, options, tests and results, decision, consequences, spec changes) is the one `docs/adr/README.md` should hold.
7. **For S2 and S3 (harness lessons, all hit here):**
   - `payload migrate` and `payload run` exit 0 without doing anything about 1 run in 20 (T14 item 4). Any helper that migrates a throwaway database must check for the table afterwards and repeat.
   - The runner's own `pkill`-style match killed the session's shell once (exit 144): match exact `ps` fields (`$2 ~ /^next-server/`), never a pattern the calling command line contains (CLOUD.md says so for `pkill -f`; it holds for `awk` over `ps` too).
   - Playwright's `click` on the admin's nav link is blocked by an overlay at 1280×720; click in the page. A toast is not proof of a save: wait for the PATCH.
   - A deep link to `/admin/logout` gave a console 400 on `POST /api/users/logout` in 2 of 6 runs; the nav link 0 of 6.
   - Cache Components off needs `src/app/global-not-found.tsx` set aside, and a request to any `use cache` route poisons the rest of that dev run (every route answers 500).

### Smaller observations

- The Gravatar request of the admin avatar fails in the cloud (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), as T09 noted; the suite excuses a failed request to another host but not one to this server.
- In dev the browser console gets Next's prerender errors with the route `"/admin/[[...segments]]"` and a frame in Payload's `RootLayout` or `NotFound`. Payload's `timestamp()` helper (the only `new Date()` I found in `@payloadcms/next`) is never called, so the source of the `new Date()` is elsewhere in Payload or Next; I did not trace it.
- Next 16.4's `experimental.instantInsights.validationLevel: 'manual-warning'` did not remove any of the dev errors, only the "instant navigation" one.
- `GET` route handlers that only read process state are prerendered unless they call `connection()`.
- The upload fields' drawer is the check for the plan's "#16270: media selection forces a full reload": it passes in `start` (6 of 6 base and W1 runs in dev too). The issue itself could not be read (the cloud blocks `github.com` issue pages), so this is my reading of it.
- Not run: Windows (W2 covers the quickstart), `partialPrefetching` with a real cart or account route (M1 onward), Instant Navigations beyond the admin.
